# Build Progress

_Last updated: 2026-07-22 (T26 full-suite verification)_

## Current status

**Phase 1–5 implemented.** Theme detection, trade ranking, position sizing, daily P&L, risk metrics, and the **Q1 reasoning pipeline** (L0–L6) are all wired into `scripts/daily_refresh.py`. The L5 agent (`backend/services/q1_agent.py`) produces a structured $100M long-short book with per-trade thesis, factor tilts, scenario analysis, and citation-verified numeric claims. The Q1 thesis renders on the `/research` page with inline citation footnotes. See "Pending" below for what's left to actually run against the deployed environment.

New `labelInside` prop (default false, `ThemeTrends` unaffected) anchors each label at the line's own endpoint (`text-anchor="end"`, extending leftward) instead of a separate right-hand column; paired with a much smaller `plotRight={16}` at the narrative board's call site, plot width grows to 370 units — arithmetic, not cosmetic. The label anti-collision stacking logic didn't change (never depended on which side labels render); only the displaced-label connector's shape changed, from a rightward polyline to a short vertical tie — the same shape `DetectionScatter` already uses. Second: `TrendPlot` colours lines by rank rotation with an `IDENTITY_COLORS[phrase]` check that never actually fires for a narrative phrase (no tracked phrase is literally "AI Capex" — the theme name lives at `covered_by`), while `DetectionScatter` colours dots through a *different* function checking `covered_by` directly — so "ai" got the AI Capex pink hue on its dot but a rotation colour on its line, an inconsistency neither chart's own tests could see. Both components now accept an optional `colors` map, checked before either's own resolution logic; `NarrativeTrends` computes ONE map from `top` — the only place holding both a phrase's rank and its `covered_by` — fixing "ai" as a byproduct rather than the point of the change. Only `top`'s handful of phrases are ever in the map, so every other mark keeps its existing coverage-only scheme untouched (ADR-0146's filled/hollow encoding stays undiluted for the long tail it actually governs), and label ink stays explicitly coverage-only (ADR-0162) — matching a mark's hue to its line is a different claim from matching its label's ink to the payload's. | 2026-07-30 | **The narrative board's two plots now share one axis, not just one width.** [ADR-0177](docs/adrs/0177-one-share-two-axes-one-scale.md). Verified live on the real 2026-07-30 run: both charts render the identical `0%, 5%, 10%, 15%, 20%, 25%, 30%` tick set. 4 new tests — `yMax`/`xMax` each proven to override the component's own computation, plus an integration test proving the shared max reaches a series' historical peak rather than clamping to whichever chart's own number happens to be smaller. **958 frontend green**, `tsc --noEmit` clean |

| 2026-07-30 | **The first live run under the new turnover cap surfaced a second, larger defect the same day it shipped — and manually triggering that run was the only way to find it.** [ADR-0174](docs/adrs/0174-a-constraint-cannot-bind-a-variable-it-was-never-given.md). `daily_refresh.py` was run manually (after snapshotting all four book tables per standing practice) to verify ADR-0173's turnover cap end-to-end. It reported `optimizer_result.realised_turnover` **60.0%** — exactly at the new cap, `"turnover at cap"` in `binding_constraints` — while `book_holdings_performance.turnover`, computed independently by `held_book.weight_delta`, measured **94.5%**. The 34.5-point gap matched, to five decimal places, five names (TLT, VRT, GDX, PDD, NOC) held on 2026-07-29 and simply absent from the 2026-07-30 candidate pool — not reweighted to zero, never a decision variable the solver had at all. `optimize()`'s constraint (`cp.norm1(w - w_held) <= max_turnover`) and its own `realised_turnover` audit both iterate `assets` — TODAY's candidate list — so a name entering is counted correctly (`w_held` defaults to 0 for it) but a name **exiting because it fell off the screen entirely** is invisible to both: the exact "iterate one side's keys" trap `held_book.weight_delta`'s own docstring names, one layer upstream, and worse than the pre-ADR-0173 state — a reader saw "60% — config" and read it as governed, when the true figure breached the cap by more than half again its own value. Fix: a dropped holding's exit is now a **forced, constant cost** subtracted from the solver's remaining budget before it solves (`remaining_budget = max(0, max_turnover - forced_exit_turnover)`), never a negative budget, since a cost the solver could not avoid is not a tighter choice, it is zero choice. `realised_turnover` now includes it, so it matches `book_holdings_performance.turnover` by construction rather than by coincidence. `forced_exit_turnover` is a new, separately-named field (`optimizer_result.forced_exit_turnover`) — 34.5 of 94.5 points was too large to fold silently into a bigger total. `binding_constraints` now distinguishes an ordinary bind (`"turnover at cap"`) from a breach forced exits caused alone (`"turnover cap breached by candidate exits alone..."`) — reporting the second as merely "at cap" would repeat the exact false-assurance failure this ADR fixes. **2 new adversarial backend tests**, both would fail pre-fix: a partial forced exit correctly shrinks (not zeroes) the solver's remaining budget; forced exits alone exceeding the cap freezes every solver-visible name and reports a breach rather than a false bind. **1437 backend green.** Left open, deliberately not decided unilaterally: the already-published 2026-07-30 `optimizer_result` still carries the wrong pre-fix numbers (the traded positions/sizes are unaffected — the bug lived in the reporting and residual budget, not the sizing choice within `assets`) — correcting an already-published diagnostic is the `book_revisions`/ADR-0093 governance question, not an optimizer one, and is for the operator to decide |
| 2026-07-30 | **The turnover cap governs the right baseline, and the covariance that sizes the book is the same one shrunk before it reports the book's risk.** [ADR-0173](docs/adrs/0173-the-turnover-cap-had-no-baseline-and-the-risk-it-reported-was-the-risk-it-optimised-against.md) + migration 059. `book_holdings_performance` measured mean daily turnover **92.7%** over 7 sessions, peak **200.0%**, cumulative cost **0.973% ($973k)**, ~**35%/yr** annualised — gross −1.26% became net −2.01%. `held_book.py`'s own docstring had already named the fix: `max_turnover` has existed since ADR-0107 and nothing ever set it. **Two defects sat underneath.** (1) The one existing test bound the constraint against `weights0` — the CONVICTION book of the SAME run, which `weight_delta` and the frontier still need as their own baseline — so even switched on it would have capped the optimizer's intra-run adjustment, not day-over-day churn: migration 056's `rebalance_cost` mistake, one layer along, where the measurement got fixed and the optimiser never re-pointed. (2) `covariance_from_returns` fed BOTH `optimize()` (minimising `w'Σw`) and the ex-post VaR/Monte-Carlo report, so the same noisy sample Σ optimised against was reported AS the book's risk — biased low by construction (Markowitz error-maximisation) — while μ was shrunk 50% (ADR-0033) and Σ received no equivalent treatment. Fix, two parts. `OptimizerInputs` gains **`weights_held`** (yesterday's PUBLISHED book, `book_holdings.signed_weight`, fetched once in `run_q1_agent` mirroring ADR-0171's own pattern so `size_positions` stays pure); the constraint becomes `‖w − w_held‖₁ ≤ max_turnover`, applied only when a prior book exists. `OptimizationResult` now reports TWO figures never conflated — `turnover` (intra-run, unchanged) and `realised_turnover`/`turnover_cap` (day-over-day, new) — and a consequence got fixed in passing: `binding_constraints` had been checking "turnover at cap" against the WRONG quantity. `Mandate.max_turnover = 0.60`: the 30–58% band (5 of 7 sessions) is unaffected, the two 141.6%/200.0% outliers are roughly halved. Second: `covariance_from_returns` shrinks toward **constant correlation** (Ledoit & Wolf 2003 target, keeping each asset's own VARIANCE) at a **fixed 0.25 intensity** — not Ledoit-Wolf's derived-optimal one, the same fixed-and-stated philosophy `IC_SHRINKAGE` already established — wired INSIDE the one function every consumer calls, so the optimizer and the risk report cannot diverge on which Σ is "the" covariance. Reported as `optimizer_result.cov_shrinkage_intensity`. **Frontend**: turnover joins the mandate as an 11th limit (two hand-built lists needed updating — `riskBoard.ts` and `MandatePanel.tsx`'s separate `ENFORCED_ROWS`), deliberately NOT gated by session history (needs none), rendering **NO DATA** rather than a false 0%/OK on the currently-published pre-deploy book. The Risk answer row's fourth card (this morning's ADR-0172) said "Σ is not shrunk at all" — now rewritten to read the actual persisted intensity per run, so a pre-ADR-0173 book still says it was genuinely unshrunk rather than claiming a mitigation it never had. Verified live: mandate panel reads `Turnover (day-over-day) — 60% — config`. **19 new backend tests** (3 adversarial to the specific historical mistake: binds-on-weights_held-not-weights0, does-not-bind-the-intra-run-figure, no-prior-book-means-unconstrained) + **7 new frontend tests**. **1435 backend / 938 frontend green.** Rejected: sklearn's `LedoitWolf` (shrinks toward IDENTITY, the wrong target — would flatten the variances risk attribution reads — and an undeclared dependency risks the exact cvxpy/gensim class of failure already hit once this session) |
| 2026-07-30 | **Every phase now answers its own question above the fold, and each block sits on the tab that asks it.** [ADR-0172](docs/adrs/0172-every-phase-answers-its-own-question-above-the-fold.md). The app was hard for a PM to decide from and the measurement says why: **25 screens, 1,444 numerals, ZERO inner scrollers** — so design-goal 7 was intact and density was NOT the fault. What was: **five of six phases had no decision surface** (`/book` had one), and **blocks sat on the tab whose question they did not answer** — `/scenario` asked *"what proves the thesis right and what do the other paths cost"* with its stress matrix at **2622px**, while `/mandate` carried *versus the benchmark* and *these weights over the past year* (both phase 6) and `/attribution` was the thinnest page in the app. The fix already existed on ONE page; `AnswerCards.tsx` states it in its own header — *"the measured defect this fixes is PLACEMENT, not paint"* — so it was extracted to `components/AnswerRow.tsx` and given to all five data-bearing phases, contract included: **LABEL / FIGURE / CONSEQUENCE**, consequence COMPUTED not canned, figure links to the panel deriving it, **four cards never five**. **Phase 3 renamed `Risk` and reclaimed `/risk`**, which simplified this morning's ADR-0170 — `#stress`/`#attribution`/`#exposure`/`#concentration` resolve natively again and the hop shrank to the three fragments that moved. Moves justified by each destination's declared `question`: cap utilisation → `/mandate`, benchmark + weights backtest → `/attribution`, track record → `/attribution`, VaR by method → `/risk`, stress scenarios → top. **Nothing collapsed, at the owner's direction** — and it proved unnecessary: the moves alone took `/mandate` **5.3 → 3.6 screens** and **232 → 122 numerals** with everything still visible, which is now recorded as a narrowing of goal 7 (check whether content BELONGS on a page before reaching for a disclosure). **Result — all seven routes pass "no phase route where the block answering its own question sits below 1000px"**, `/risk` **2622 → 576px**. **Three findings the rows surfaced that nothing had aggregated:** only **4 of 10 limits come from `scoring_config`** (six are house defaults — a cap that binds but cannot be attributed); **the same Σ sizes the book and reports its risk**, so reported vol is a **lower bound** while μ is shrunk 50% and Σ not at all (now on `/risk`'s fourth card; the shrinkage is Part B); and `/` was reading `narrative_signals` **twice**. **Two mistakes worth keeping:** 929 tests passed with `RiskBody` syntactically broken and every page 500ing — its only test reads the file with `readFileSync`, so nothing imports it and vitest structurally cannot catch it, while tsc can; and lifting `useNarrativeSeries` to the page took fetches **2 → 6**, because a hook cannot be called conditionally so the `shared` prop chose which RESULT displayed and removed no read. The hook took `{ skip }` and it measured 2. **The prop alone was the plausible fix and it was the wrong one.** 929/929 |
| 2026-07-30 | **Editorial judgment exists, and it acts forward because a track record cannot be edited.** [ADR-0171](docs/adrs/0171-a-veto-acts-forward-because-a-track-record-cannot-be-edited.md) + migration 058. An outside review named four Q1 gaps; **two were factually wrong** — the portfolio thesis and the deliberate pairs both exist and RENDER on `/book` via `ThesisBlock` (`BookBody.tsx:945`), and the live 07-29 `book_view` states *"the MSFT short paired with VRT and GEV longs expresses a hardware-over-software rotation within the AI Capex theme"*. One was fair: every filter in `screen_candidates` encodes a measurable property (HypeScore, `|EdgeScore|`, factor R², lens, complex caps) and **none can express "the attention on this name is one news cycle, not a signal"**. **The obvious fix is the one that must not be built.** `pick_outcomes` sets `entry = close on run_date` (ADR-0090) and `resolve_outcomes.py:123` re-derives its claim set from CURRENT `picks` — so editing a published book would let a substitute inherit an entry price from before the decision (a swap on 07-30 into the 07-29 book scores from 07-29's close: one day of free hindsight) **and** silently stop resolving the removed name. That is a track record an operator could launder by editing history, in a product whose whole claim is an auditable trail. So `editorial_vetoes` acts on the NEXT run and never touches a published row. `reason` is **NOT NULL by constraint** (the `book_revisions` guard, same reasoning — an unexplained absence is indistinguishable from a screen bug); `direction` NULL means both sides, because *"untradeable"* and *"this SHORT is crowded"* are different claims. Applied **before the cap-30** so a refused name cannot occupy an LLM context slot and push a good candidate out by proxy, and **after the rule filters** so vetoing an already-dropped name cannot double-count attrition. Surfaces as a `screening_funnel` stage shaped like ADR-0046's override, **rendered even when it removed nothing** and quoting reasons **verbatim** (the reason IS the judgement; paraphrasing authors a string nobody wrote). Rows are **revoked, never deleted**. `scripts/veto.py` is the operator CLI — add / list / revoke / dry-run — because without a write path the table is schema nobody can use. **Three things caught in verification and worth keeping:** the fetch initially sat INSIDE `screen_candidates` and broke two existing tests with `KeyError: supabase_url`, so it moved to `run_q1_agent` and the node stayed pure; the `dry-run` output crashed on a cp1252 console printing U+2212, the exact trap `resolve_outcomes.py:152` already documents; and the veto was proven to bite against the live 36-name pool (`- GDX short: ... (claude-smoke-test)`) before the smoke-test rows were removed. Costs recorded: **a veto cannot help today's book**; a read failure degrades to *no vetoes applied* rather than *refuse everything*, so one can silently fail to apply; and **determinism (ADR-0013) now has a human input** — reproducible given the veto set, but no longer a pure function of market data. **1427 backend / 915 frontend green** |
| 2026-07-30 | **The phase strip fades its ends — but only when it actually overflows.** Follow-up to [ADR-0170](docs/adrs/0170-the-navigation-is-the-process.md), whose own cost note said six tabs scroll on a narrow viewport. Measured: at 900px the track overflows by **187px**, so two phases sat outside it with nothing to say so — a nav that reads as HAVING four items rather than showing four of six. Two rejected shapes are the reason `scrollFadeMask` is a function rather than a class: a **static right-edge fade** dims `06 Attribution` at every width where all six already fit, which reads as *disabled* rather than as *continued* and is worse than the problem; and an **overlay gradient** would have to fake `bg-bg-primary/85` + `backdrop-blur` and be wrong over any scrolled content behind it, where a `mask-image` fades the CONTENT and needs to know nothing about the backdrop. So the mask is null unless overflow exceeds 1px (fractional layout widths make `scrollWidth` exceed `clientWidth` on a strip that visually fits), and it **swaps ends** with scroll position, because once scrolled right the hidden items are on the LEFT. `ResizeObserver` and not a window listener: this nav is a grid track whose width changes when the tool cluster beside it grows (`Next run 21:30 UTC` appears at `md`, and the run-state cell widens once data loads) with no window resize involved. Verified 1360px → no mask, 900px → right only, scrolled to end → left only with `06` fully opaque, mid-scroll → both. **915/915.** Also recorded: `next build` and `next dev` share `.next` and corrupt each other in BOTH directions — a build under a live dev server leaves the server serving `Cannot find module './682.js'` on every route, and a build while the server is running fails with `ENOENT` collecting page data. Neither is a code fault and both cost time this session |
| 2026-07-30 | **Navigation IS the process — one tab per phase, and `/risk` split three ways to allow it.** [ADR-0170](docs/adrs/0170-the-navigation-is-the-process.md), superseding ADR-0169's navigation decision at the owner's direction. `TopBar` and `SideRail` render `PHASES` in order — **01 Mandate · 02 Alpha · 03 Scenario · 04 Construction · 05 Execution · 06 Attribution** — from the same array `/method` renders, so strip, rail and map cannot drift. ADR-0169 had considered and REJECTED this; what changed is not the analysis but the priority, since the sequence is what this artefact demonstrates and a numbered strip teaches it before a reader has read a word. **Two of that rejection's three objections survive as costs and are paid rather than argued away.** `/risk` answered phases 1, 3 and 6 at once, and a tab strip cannot mark three of its own tabs current — so the body moved to `components/risk/RiskBody.tsx` and is filtered by `phaseShows` across `/mandate`, `/scenario` and `/attribution`, keeping **ONE `useEffect` and one set of queries** (the `MethodBody`+`chapterOwns` shape: three fetches are three chances to describe different vintages of one run). Two placements are deliberate and easy to invert — **`limits` sits with the MANDATE**, because the board measures against the panel above it, and **`attribution` (per-position risk decomposition) sits with SCENARIO**, because it is ex-ante while phase 6 is ex-post and the shared word disguises two different questions. `/risk` survives as a **fragment-aware hop rather than a redirect**: a server redirect cannot see a hash, so it forwards `/risk#stress` → `/scenario#stress` client-side, and in-repo links were repointed directly so they do not flash-and-hop. **`/execution` is a route with no figures on purpose** — a sequence skipping 4 to 6 reads as a missing page, and per ADR-0040 the book is a recommendation, so no fill, borrow cost or slippage exists to report. `/method` and `/risk` left the nav; `PhaseChip` deleted. **`SideRail` needed a third label**: 56px and un-wideable (ADR-0086), ~9 characters at 10px, and `Construction` clipped on the live rail — `Phase.short` fixes it with a **word, not a number**, since the rail's rule is icon AND label and "04" does not tell a reader where it goes. Three details that matter: `S_WIDTH` is pinned near the **narrowest** column the card will offer so the plot only ever scales UP (below 1.0 labels shrink, above it they grow, and only one direction is recoverable); **`max-w-[520px]` bounds the scale**, since a plain `w-full` renders the plane at full card width whenever the columns stack (~1134px below the `figures` gate) = **2.98×** with 9px labels at 27px, reading as a blown-up detail crop; and the figures column is **fixed at 340px, not a fraction**, because it has a hard 319px minimum and no use for more, so fixing it hands every remaining pixel to the plane. **Also folded in — one reading of the series** (`lib/useNarrativeSeries.ts`): the corpus (ADR-0153), the last-measured-day rule (ADR-0159) and the `asOfFallback` disclosure move out of both components into a hook they share. Asked to draw it, the obvious execution is one proportional funnel, width ∝ count — and that is **wrong**, because a funnel's visual grammar is *subset-of*. Queried live, `themes.promotion_basis` (migration 051 / ADR-0134) is **8 `practitioner_prior`** — migration 001's opening list, which predates the tracker and was never in its corpus — **1 `operator_directed`** (AI Capex), **0 `measured_discovery`**. A signal that prompts a question is not evidence that justified an answer. Rebuilt as **two labelled halves** (`Observed · narrative_signals` ‖ `Committed · themes`): proportional bars inside the observed half **only**; the committed half as **nine pips on no shared scale**, one filled, so it cannot be read as a fraction of 193; **a rule between them, never an arrow**; the basis counts rendered by value — and **the zero is the informative figure**, since no theme on this board was found by the pipeline before a human named it. `practitioner_prior` is deliberately *not* framed as a deficiency (migration 051: a prior is a legitimate way to start a universe, and NULL evidence there is correct rather than missing). ADR-0066 unchanged and still binding: with no measurable velocity the emerging stage is a **dashed track with no width**, never a zero-length bar — which is precisely what a bar chart's default rendering of a null looks like. **A layout bug caught in the browser rather than reasoned about:** the first draft sized each bar as a percentage of the flex ROW, so with two fixed-width siblings `width: 100%` overshot the column and painted the longest bar straight over the committed half; the bar now sizes against its own `flex-1 min-w-0` track. **The page order deliberately does NOT change** — asked whether the Narrative card should precede all Theme cards since the funnel runs narrative → theme, the answer is no: reordering `/` to match a chronology that produced *one* of nine themes would encode the same false derivation in layout form, and the narrative board is shadow and sizes nothing (ADR-0128), so leading with it ranks pipeline chronology over reader value. Nothing had ever driven that loop (ten arguments, Supabase persistence), which is why the contradiction survived a green suite. ADR-0052 was right about the cost and wrong about the remedy: the same shape appears at **two** ceilings -- 420 and 900 -- and both times attempt 2 succeeded, on 2026-07-29 producing a real ten-pick book with 45 citations. The retry is what rescues the book. Now `MAX_TIMEOUT_RETRIES = 1` against `MAX_REASON_RETRIES = 2`; citation failures keep their full budget. Loop extracted to `reason_and_verify_with_retries` so it is testable -- reverting the constant fails 4 tests, verified not assumed. Also per-node timings, because `pipeline_runs` recorded only the whole-stage duration and "several calls" vs "one burned deadline plus a retry" imply opposite fixes; and **line-buffered stdout**, because a redirected run showed 21 lines for 45 minutes while L0-L5 finished behind them, making a hung job indistinguishable from a slow one. 1378 backend green. |

The backfill bucketed by publication; the nightly job selected on `run_date` — and one GDELT call returns 45 days of history, so all 1449 articles carry today's `run_date`. Measured, they spanned **32 publication days**. Fixed by bucketing on publication date everywhere, through **one** implementation (`replay_archive`, called by both the script and the nightly job). Fixing it exposed the layer beneath: replayed properly, the newest days hold 9–16 documents against a median of 49, so the guard withheld them for being too *small* — ADR-0154 fetched GDELT oldest-first on the premise that "Brave already covers the recent end", but the archive is **GDELT-only by definition**, so Brave covers none of it. GDELT now fetches the **newest window first**, then the rest oldest-first: truncation costs middle days, which are recoverable because GDELT is an archive, where the live end is not. | 2026-07-29 | **The archive was not sparse — it was truncated.** [ADR-0154](docs/adrs/0154-maxrecords-caps-a-response-not-a-query.md): `maxrecords` caps a RESPONSE, not a query, so one request spanning 45 days returned 250 articles for the **whole window** (~5.5/day) while the same query over 7 days also returns 250 (**34/day**). Both at the cap — the remainder was never missing from GDELT, it was discarded before reaching us. Six times the density from asking for less at a time, which is the difference between `MIN_DOC_COUNT = 3` being a **27%** share of the day (only newswire register clears it) and **9%**. Windows run oldest-first so a truncated fetch loses the recent end, which Brave already covers at 87-94/day. This also re-reads ADR-0153's evidence: 1 query -> 232, 4 -> 462, 10 -> the same 462 looked like query redundancy, and the dominant effect was every query being truncated identically. Budget 200 -> 600s, and the distinction matters more than the number — it was raised to 420s hours earlier on an **inference** the measurement falsified, and reverted; windowing multiplies the request count for real. Two bugs the loop introduced, both caught by tests that predate it: a query admitted by the outer budget check could fetch **nothing**, and a permanently-rejected query was retried once per window. 1314 backend green. |

| 2026-07-28 | **Wired GDELT as a second news provider. The first live run returned zero headlines, and that failure was worth more than the success.** [ADR-0144](docs/adrs/0144-a-second-provider-that-is-an-archive.md). Two open findings had one fix: [ADR-0094](docs/adrs/0094-no-corroboration-gate-over-a-single-source.md) disclosed that the attention signal rests on **one** provider, and [ADR-0143](docs/adrs/0143-the-price-link-gate-and-what-it-refuses-to-say.md)'s price-link gate needed ~20 trading sessions and had 5. Asked whether Brave could backfill the history -- **measured, and no**: on identical 45-day windows Brave puts **48% of its documents in the last 7 days** with older days holding 1-5 each, against GDELT's **18%** with older days holding 3-9. Brave is a recency *ranking*; GDELT is an *archive*. The decisive argument was not thinness but that **39 Brave dates would clear the 20-session floor with fabricated density**, flipping `insufficient_history` into a confident false `linked` -- **the backfill would defeat the guard by satisfying it**, which is `backfill_regime.py`'s standing refusal to reconstruct HypeScore, now with numbers. **Then the first live run through my own client returned 0 headlines from 3 queries**, logging *"throttled or non-JSON (HTTP 200): Queries containing OR'd terms must be surrounded by ()."* Two defects behind one message: **(1)** GDELT requires OR'd terms in parentheses and Brave does not, so every multi-term seed query was rejected -- `gdelt_query()` now translates, idempotently; **(2)** the client treated a **permanent query error as throttling**, retrying something that could never succeed through the full 15+30+60s ladder and reporting the wrong cause, so the real error never reached the log. **Neither was reachable by the 16 unit tests, which stub the response body and therefore cannot discover what the server actually says** -- the suite was green throughout. After the fix a live run returns **231 headlines across 40 distinct days** (2026-06-14 to 07-27), English, having recovered from a genuine 429 through the retry path. Four other constraints are encoded because each was learned by tripping it: 1 request/5s server-enforced with the pacing in the client (a rate limit honoured only when the caller remembers is not one, and tripping it cost ~60s of cooldown, not 5); `sourcelang:english` appended here, because without it the corpus filled with *"L'integrale de BFM Bourse"*; explicit UTF-8 decoding, because inference produced mojibake; and a **wall-clock budget**, because the retry ladder can burn ~131s per query and ~22 minutes for ten against a ten-minute pipeline -- at expiry it returns what it has and reports how many queries it skipped. GDELT **supplements** Brave (39 docs/day recent vs 6, but 45 days of them) and a GDELT failure is caught as *"the corpus loses its history, not its present"*. Expectation, not result: `insufficient_history` should clear next run, since 45 days is ~31 sessions against a floor of 20. 24 tests, 1238 backend total. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Built the price-link gate. The first live run returned `linked` for every phrase, which is why it now refuses to answer at all.** [ADR-0143](docs/adrs/0143-the-price-link-gate-and-what-it-refuses-to-say.md). ADR-0142 argued the narrative-vs-register problem is **economic, not linguistic** -- the brief defines a theme as *a narrative driving cross-asset moves* -- and then said the gate could not be built yet for want of history. **That was wrong, and checking rather than assuming found it**: `market_news` carries `published_date`, and its 295 rows span **8 publication dates**, so a daily mention series is reconstructable from stored headlines today. Built as the SAME statistic the nine anchors already face -- ADR-0127's `correlation_with_mentions` -> `per_class_corr` -> `corr_breadth`, [ADR-0064](docs/adrs/0064-one-formula-one-place.md)'s one-formula-one-place -- so a phrase is judged by exactly the test the live themes are, which is what makes them comparable. Series is **share of voice per publication date**, not a raw count: collection volume swings 13-to-80 across the window (weekend dip), and raw counts would correlate with the news cycle rather than the phrase. **Then the live run rewrote the design.** It returned `linked` for **all fourteen** phrases -- scores 0.84-1.00, 4-5 of 5 asset classes material -- with `earnings`, `wall`, `q2` and `analysts` all apparently moving the entire book. Cause: `sessions = 5`, because 8 publication days across a weekend is five overlapping trading sessions, and at n=5 a Pearson r sits near ±1 by chance. **The gate was measuring its own sample size.** My first cut computed that, returned `linked`, and exposed a separate `provisional` flag -- which is precisely [ADR-0059](docs/adrs/0059-a-single-date-ic-is-not-validation.md)'s failure shape, the true answer behind an opt-in a caller must remember to check ([ADR-0100](docs/adrs/0100-a-guard-that-lives-in-a-component-guards-one-consumer.md)). So **`insufficient_history` is now the verdict itself** below 20 sessions, with two deliberately separate floors: **5 to compute** (`correlation_with_mentions` refuses below it) and **20 to believe** (about a trading month; at n=20 an |r| of 0.44 clears p<0.05, a bar a real relationship reaches and noise mostly does not). Raw subscore, per-class correlations and session count all stay on the record -- refusing a verdict is not hiding a measurement, and a reader can see `earnings` scored 0.93 on five sessions and judge that themselves. **It currently returns `insufficient_history` for everything, which is the correct output rather than a failure**, and becomes useful around **2026-08-17**. It gates nothing yet by construction: wiring it in now would pass everything or block everything. `rank_by_link` (linked before ambient, breadth before magnitude, `insufficient_history` sunk to the bottom) is written and unused for the same reason. Stated: correlation is not causation and this cannot tell them apart -- prices moving may generate the coverage; the claim supported is the weaker sufficient one, that a phrase has *something* to do with a tradeable asset rather than being ambient. And the 8-day window is **one fetch's view of the past week**, not eight daily collections -- search ranking favours recency, so older days are under-represented until the daily corpus accumulates. 19 tests, 1217 backend total. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Asked whether a TF-IDF baseline would separate narratives from financial boilerplate. Measured it: no -- and two other candidates fail too.** [ADR-0142](docs/adrs/0142-frequency-cannot-tell-a-narrative-from-a-register.md), following [ADR-0141](docs/adrs/0141-the-corpus-and-its-denominator-were-both-ours.md). With the corpus bias fixed the board read `earnings` 27.6%, `price` 13.7%, `q2` 10.6%, `growth`, `revenue`, `analysts` -- with `ai` at **#12**. That is the *register* of financial writing, not a set of narratives. **TF-IDF produced a byte-identical top-10 to share**, and provably so rather than by accident: this counts DOCUMENT frequency (`phrases_in` returns a set), so `tfidf = x·log(1/x) = -x·ln(x)`, which is **monotonically increasing for x < 1/e = 0.368**. Every phrase sits far below that, so TF-IDF is an order-preserving transform of share and **cannot reorder anything** -- it exists to damp term-frequency inflation, which was designed out on day one. **Background lift** ranked `q2` 211x, `revenue` 184x, `guidance` 143x, all with background count **zero**, because the only accumulated corpus is `theme_news` (Fed/dollar/oil/China) and it never discusses earnings -- ADR-0141's bias reappearing one layer along. **Capitalisation** looked like a free entity signal (`Alphabet` is a name, `earnings` is a description) and also failed on measurement: **64% of headlines are title-cased**, where casing carries nothing, and the sentence-cased remainder yields `by`, `investing.com`, `the`, `times` as its top entity candidates. **What actually discriminates is change, then price.** Velocity is already built and is self-referenced against a phrase's own history, so unlike a background it needs no representative baseline -- `earnings` at 27% *every day* has zero velocity and classifies `established`. Beyond that, the brief's own definition: a market theme is *a narrative driving cross-asset moves*, so the decisive test is whether attention to a phrase co-moves with prices -- `correlation_with_mentions` and ADR-0127's per-class machinery already answer exactly that for anchors. **The generalisable point: this was being attacked as a linguistics problem when the definition is economic.** Frequency, TF-IDF, stoplists and capitalisation all try to decide "is this word a theme" from text alone; a word is a market theme when attention to it moves prices. **Nothing added** -- both instruments need the same input (accumulated `market_news`, now 295 docs/day), velocity reports after ~4 runs, and the stoplist is deliberately NOT grown because hand-curating a register list is ADR-0128's hard-coded-themes problem in miniature. Falsifiable in four runs: `ai` carries positive velocity while `earnings` sits near zero; if `earnings` spikes too, the price-link gate becomes necessary rather than merely better. **Also fixed: my ADR numbers collided again.** I had referenced 0139 and 0140 in committed code; both are the concurrent session's (`dollar-debasement-indicator`, `hawkish-dovish-pivot-indicator`, and theirs cite my 0127/0128, so they are building on this). Renumbered every reference to 0141 across five files. |

| 2026-07-28 | **Ran the pipeline to check the thesis fix, and the run surfaced a unit bug where the prompt and the guardrail contradicted each other.** [ADR-0137](docs/adrs/0137-basis-points-are-not-a-hundredfold-error.md). **The thesis check first:** the new book is 10 picks and **MSFT short is now in it** -- the pair VRT's thesis described last run and did not hold is complete, VRT long 7.7% against MSFT short 0.4%, both theses describing the same relative-value structure (*"long the picks-and-shovels, short the spender"*), **zero unheld position claims**, verified by an independent audit rather than by the absent log line. Two honest limits: the **repair code never fired** because there was nothing to fix, so this run exercised the detector and the prompt but not the excision path; and I cannot claim the prompt *caused* it, since "don't claim a leg you didn't take" is satisfiable by not claiming or by taking the leg, and it took the leg -- consistent with the fix working and equally with a different draw on a name whose edge was already the theme's most convicted. **Then the unit bug.** The run logged *"tolerated 2/42 ungrounded citations... Cited value 279.0 does not match source 'BAMLH0A0HYM2'=2.79"*. The model was **right** -- 279bp is the high-yield OAS and FRED serves it in percentage points -- and worse, **the prompt's own worked example asks for exactly the form the verifier rejects** (`{"source": "BAMLH0A0HYM2", "value": 380.0}` against a series storing 3.80). A model following instructions precisely produced an ungrounded citation every time, and it survived only inside the 20% allowance, so **the tolerance budget was being spent on the guardrail's own contradiction** instead of on real model error. The frontend has reconciled this since `formatSlopeBps`; the backend never learned it -- the same confusion [ADR-0091](docs/adrs/0091-breadth-must-be-a-share-of-something-named.md) found in the regime classifier, one layer down. **The fix turns on spreads vs levels**, which is the entire safety argument: a spread is a difference between two yields and is universally quoted in bps, a level is quoted in percent and nobody says "469bps" for the ten-year. So `BPS_QUOTED_SOURCES` lists HY OAS, IG OAS and the curve slope, and **deliberately excludes** DGS10/DGS2/DFII10/T10YIE/VIXCLS where a 100x claim is still an error and still fails. The obvious implementation -- a blanket "also try /100" -- would have accepted an order-of-magnitude error on **every** series in the snapshot, turning the guardrail against fabricated magnitudes into one that waves them through. What was added is not a wider tolerance but a **per-source declaration of a domain fact**, each entry carrying its reason so a careless addition has to argue for itself. The **prompt is fixed too** (leaving the check to absorb a contradiction the instruction creates treats the symptom), and reconciliations are **reported, not silent**, because an accepted conversion and an exact match are different events. **Replayed against the live run: exactly the two tolerated citations now pass** -- HY OAS 279/2.79 and IG OAS 80/0.8, both spreads -- source-key matches 19 -> 21. Stated: the 20% tolerance was **masking** this, which is what a failure budget does by design; and the stored unit itself is unchanged, because normalising to bps would touch the classifier, the frontend formatters and every persisted derivation, and would break the one layer already handling it correctly. 1181 backend tests. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Fixed the thesis rather than footnoting it -- and the diagnosis corrected ADR-0135's account of the cause.** [ADR-0136](docs/adrs/0136-repair-the-thesis-rather-than-footnote-it.md). ADR-0135 caveated VRT's *"long VRT / short MSFT structure"* against a book with no MSFT, and deferred the real fix as "a change to L5's control flow". Making it meant first checking what that ADR **assumed**: where the leg was lost. `research_agent_runs.raw_output` settles it -- the model's raw picks were **already** the final nine, and **MSFT was never emitted**. Nothing downstream dropped a leg. ADR-0135's *"did not survive selection"* was right about the symptom and wrong about the mechanism, which matters because it moves the fix: a repair aimed at *preserving* a leg would have targeted a stage that never lost one. **Two changes.** The **prompt** now forbids asserting a position in any ticker outside the model's own picks, cites the 2026-07-28 failure as the example, and explicitly preserves the legal case (*"you may still COMPARE... you may not claim a leg you did not take"*) so it does not suppress the correct GDX-style cluster prose. And **`repair_thesis` excises the offending clause deterministically** -- not a second LLM call, for three reasons that compound: re-prompting spends the nightly book's shared MiniMax quota (the constraint that gates `/ask` at all), it reruns a **stochastic** step over an output whose figures are already verified so a correct book can come back worse, and it can introduce **new numbers** that then need re-verifying -- turning a prose fix into another citation problem. Excision only ever removes text, so every surviving figure was already checked and [ADR-0013](docs/adrs/0013-determinism.md)'s determinism holds. **The repair declines more often than it fires, which is the design**: sentence/semicolon boundaries only (a comma cut leaves *"Vertiv, the picks-and-shovels beneficiary,"* dangling), at least 55% of the original must survive or the clause was carrying the argument rather than decorating it, and a single-clause thesis is never cut -- *"We are short MSFT on decelerating cloud growth"* is left alone with a caveat saying the text stands and why. **The edit is never silent**: [ADR-0093](docs/adrs/0093-a-published-book-that-changes-must-say-so.md)'s rule that a published figure cannot change without saying so, applied to published prose -- the caveat names the ticker, **quotes the removed text verbatim**, and affirms the remaining figures are unchanged and verified. Also closes the punctuation seam, because cutting a trailing clause left *"...electrical infrastructure;"* and a thesis that looks truncated invites doubt about the numbers in it. **Live book: 1 claim before, 0 after**, with all four verified figures (2.08, 0.30, 53.6, 81.82) retained and the prose reading as a finished argument. One test of mine had to be rewritten: it asserted the repaired token set was a subset of the original and broke the instant the seam legitimately rewrote a semicolon to a full stop -- a test asserting the **mechanism** instead of the **property**, now comparing extracted numerals. Stated: the prompt change is **unverifiable until the next run** (whether MiniMax obeys is a behavioural claim no test here settles -- the repair is the backstop precisely because instructions are not guarantees); the detector is still lexical, so *"positioned against Microsoft"* passes; and the already-published 2026-07-28 row is **not** backfilled, because editing a published artefact outside the `book_revisions` path is a bigger change than this one. 1166 backend tests. |
| 2026-07-28 | **"There are no AI stocks chosen?" — two were, and reading their theses to answer found a published thesis describing a trade the book does not hold.** [ADR-0135](docs/adrs/0135-a-thesis-must-not-assert-a-position-the-book-does-not-hold.md). **The answer first:** `AI Capex` scored **53.6** on its first scored run, cleared the 50 attention gate, produced **9 candidates**, and put **GEV long (3.7%)** and **VRT long (5.6%)** in the book — the *electrical plant* link of the chain. The agent had SMH (conviction 14.1), TSM (12.8), MSFT (14.9) and IEF (94.1) in front of it, all past the context cap, and chose picks-and-shovels over semis; its stated reason for GEV was *"independent of VRT per pool depth"*, the independent-ideas check working. **MSFT came out SHORT** at EdgeScore −0.322 — the engine disagreeing with its own theme's direction, which is ADR-0038's per-asset rule doing its job. **The defect:** VRT's published thesis argues *"the long VRT / **short MSFT** structure below isolates the capex-deployment vs capex-monetization gap"* — and MSFT is not in the book. It was a real candidate that did not survive selection, so the agent **reasoned about a pair and published one leg**. Every number in that sentence is correct, which is precisely why it passed: the citation guardrail verifies cited **numbers** against L0–L4 and has **no notion of a position**. [ADR-0049](docs/adrs/0049-thesis-badge-certifies-figures-not-prose.md) already recorded that the badge certifies figures not prose — correct for *interpretation*, but *"the book holds a short MSFT"* is not an interpretation, it is a checkable claim about our own output. **The design hinges on claim vs comparison.** A naive check fires on correct writing: the same run's GDX thesis says it is *"the strongest expression of the gold-short trade from the {GDX, GLD, IAU, NEM, SLV} cluster"* — naming a correlation complex and which member won, [ADR-0116](docs/adrs/0116-the-optimizer-chooses-the-instrument-the-thesis-argues-the-idea.md) being **explained**, asserting no position. So braced cluster listings are masked, and only a direction word attached to a ticker (`short MSFT`) or a position noun following it (`MSFT structure`) counts; one claim per ticker (that phrase matches both forms — counting twice would make one defect read as two); a pick's own asset exempt. **It warns rather than rejecting**: a citation failure means a number is wrong and the book is unsafe, this means one clause over-claims while every figure is sound, and the caveat states that the figures ARE verified so it does not overstate. Written as `thesis_caveat` **on the pick**, so /book, /ask and MCP read it from one payload rather than a log line; a clean pick is left untouched, so an absent key means "nothing over-claimed" and never "not checked". `apply_caveats` was extracted as a pure function after the first cut lived inline in `verify_citations` — testing a defect is CAUGHT should not require constructing a passing citation set for an unrelated guardrail. **Verified against the live book: exactly one finding, VRT → MSFT**, the other eight clean. Stated and not fixed: this **labels** the behaviour rather than preventing it — the agent can still lose a leg in selection, and feeding the final pick set back for a thesis rewrite is a change to L5's control flow not made here; and the detector is **lexical by choice**, because a guardrail that itself needs a model can hallucinate. 1155 backend tests. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Closed the provenance gap the AI question exposed: a theme now records whether a measurement preceded it.** [ADR-0134](docs/adrs/0134-a-prior-and-a-discovery-are-different-claims.md), migration 051. Before this, every row in `themes` read `source = 'practitioner'` with `discovered_at = NULL` — **all nine** — so `AI Capex`, created that afternoon on a measured signal plus an explicit instruction, was byte-identical in provenance to `Fed Policy`, a line in migration 001 from an opening list. On a product whose whole claim is that any number can be interrogated, the **origin of a theme** — the thing that decides which numbers get computed at all — was the one fact carrying no provenance. Three columns: `promotion_basis`, `promoted_on`, `promotion_evidence`. `source` is deliberately NOT reused; it answers *which method named it*, this answers *did evidence come first*, and overloading one column with two questions is how `price_corr` came to mean two things ([ADR-0127](docs/adrs/0127-the-cross-asset-term-measured-one-asset.md)). Four values: `practitioner_prior`, `operator_directed`, `measured_discovery`, `unrecorded`. **AI Capex is recorded `operator_directed`, not `measured_discovery`, and that is the entire point.** The strong retrospective number — AI/capex in 4 of 8 LDA topics over the 206-doc un-themed corpus, including `alphabet / capex / fund / guidance / managers / meta` — is stored under a `retrospective` key **beside** an `at_promotion` key holding the 2-of-455 signal that actually preceded the call. A payload recording only the strong number would have laundered the ordering into something it was not. **A prior with no evidence is not a defect**: `evidenceExpected()` returns false for `practitioner_prior`, so the UI never renders its null payload with the "not recorded" treatment reserved for a decision that claimed evidence and cannot show it — [ADR-0066](docs/adrs/0066-absent-is-not-zero.md)'s rule applied to a justification rather than a number. And **the chip is a label, not a ranking**: only `measured_discovery` takes emphasis, verified in a render — `PRACTITIONER PRIOR` and `OPERATOR-DIRECTED` are both neutral grey, so Fed Policy does not read as a worse theme than one the pipeline found. The **ordering caveat is derived**, not asserted: `orderingCaveat()` reads the payload shape, treating a `retrospective` block on a non-`measured_discovery` basis as the caveat whether or not anyone wrote one, because a hand-set flag could claim "evidence-led" about a payload that is not. **Nothing is `measured_discovery` yet** — the value exists and is unused, which is the honest state: no theme on this board was surfaced by the pipeline with evidence preceding promotion, and the 2026-08-01 discovery run is the first chance to earn it. Stated and not fixed: the column is self-reported, so a careless author can enter the wrong basis; a guard comparing `promoted_on` against the evidence's `measured_on` would be a test of nothing with one populated row. 11 tests in `theme-origin.test.ts`; 702 frontend, tsc clean. Capture `docs/captures/2026-07-28/theme-promotion-basis.png`. |

The plan was small: where the daily frequency tracker and the monthly LDA∩embedding job name the same narrative, record that both saw it. Checking the live candidate set before building it found **three of eleven candidates built on publisher names, two of them Tier 2** — the tier that exists to mean *two independent methods agreed*. `dollar / forecast / **fxstreet**` and `nato / **pravda** / ukraine`. They agreed on a byline. `theme_discovery.preprocess` had kept its own tokenizer, carrying all three defects fixed in `narrative_tracker` hours earlier: no attribution stripping, no publisher stoplist, and `len(t) > 2`, which deletes `ai` — so the discovery job **could not have found the AI capex narrative even with a perfect corpus**. Building corroboration on top would have laundered publisher noise into the strongest badge the system emits, so the source was fixed first: `preprocess` now delegates to `narrative_tracker.tokenize`. One tokenizer, one stoplist — [ADR-0064](docs/adrs/0064-one-formula-one-place.md)'s rule applied to a wordlist, and the two implementations diverged precisely because they were two. **That also closed a live hole in my own ADR-0130**: `preprocess` did `from gensim... import STOPWORDS` **inside** a function that ADR called pure, so `cluster_term_sets` still failed without the ML stack — and CI, which deliberately does not install gensim, **would have gone red on the next push, caused by ADR-0130's own removal of the `--ignore`**. A one-token phrase can never be corroborated — it cannot share two tokens with anything — which is deliberate and the same argument as the single-token attribution rule. The claim is **not** "two votes beat one": the methods **fail differently** (frequency is fooled by repeated boilerplate, clustering by a coherent-but-tiny topic), so neither failure explains both. Safety properties: corroboration re-persists *after* the tracker has already written, so a failure costs the badge and never the day's signals; an unmatched signal is returned **bit-identical**, the property that makes it safe on a live board ([ADR-0110](docs/adrs/0110-crowding-caps-what-it-can-see.md)); only the **newest** discovery run is read, because unioning several would let a candidate dropped by a later run silently resurrect. And `data` stopped being a stopword: it read as newswire furniture and was silently destroying **"data center"** — n-grams are built over the *filtered* stream, so a furniture judgement on one common token can delete a theme. **Two tests inverted, and the inversion is the point**: `covered_by is None` for `ai capex cycle` was true and was the finding; it now asserts `== "AI Capex"`, because the phrase left the emerging shortlist by being **adopted**. So Fed Policy, mapped across eight instruments spanning rates/commodities/equity, was scored on whichever ticker Postgres happened to return first under `order(run_date desc).limit(20)` — an ordering nobody chose; and a theme moving four asset classes together scored **identically** to one tracking a single ETF. The worse half: an unmeasurable first ticker wrote a hard **0.0**, a 30-point deduction for a gap in our data, while seven mapped instruments had full history. Same shape as [ADR-0121](docs/adrs/0121-the-fix-missed-the-map-that-mattered-and-the-guard-said-clean.md) — **a guard that cannot fail reads as a check that passed.** Now `correlation_with_mentions` returns **None**, never 0.0, so the distinction is un-ignorable at the call site rather than a convention a caller may forget; every mapped instrument is measured and collapsed **per asset class** (strongest signed reading wins within a class — two rates ETFs are one reading of the rates complex, not two votes); the sub-score is the mean over **measured** classes of `min(1, |c|/0.50)`, pricing breadth and magnitude together; and the denominator counts classes *measured*, never classes *mapped* — [ADR-0036](docs/adrs/0036-edge-renormalisation.md)'s rule applied to a second formula. `hype_score` renormalises when correlation is absent, but the all-four-present arithmetic is **unchanged**, so `/method` still reconciles byte-for-byte. **HypeScores will move on the next run in both directions, and the move is the correction.** **Then the larger one: eight themes, hard-coded twice** — rows in `themes`, keyword lists in `THEME_KEYWORDS` — and attention measured by asking Brave for those keywords. A narrative matching none of the eight lists was not low-scoring, it was **structurally invisible**: no counter, no series, no row. Against the brief's own examples: *central bank pivot* is covered (US-only — no ECB or BoJ theme, PBOC buried inside China Growth), *dollar debasement* only partially (the keywords ask for the cyclical dollar, not the fiscal/de-dollarisation framing that makes debasement distinct), and **AI capex cycle not at all**. `theme_discovery.py` cannot close this and says so in its own docstring: its corpus is `theme_news`, collected **by those eight queries**, so it can only surface sub-themes of what was already named — and its SBERT/UMAP/HDBSCAN/LDA stack **is not installed in CI**, where `import gensim` fails and has been failing 14 tests. So: `MARKET_SEED_QUERIES` collects an **un-themed** corpus into `market_news` (a separate table on purpose — merging it under a sentinel theme row would feed un-themed headlines into L5's *per-theme* reasoning context as evidence about a theme that did not collect them), and `narrative_tracker.py` tracks every 1–3-gram's **share of voice** daily with a robust velocity against its own history. And a **two-letter** minimum token length, because three deleted `ai`, and with it every phrase the AI capex narrative is expressed in — *the single example the brief names* — along with `eu`, `uk`, `em`, `hy`, `qt`. `covered_by` labels each phrase with the anchor already asking for it, which is what makes the output answer the question: a surge in `fomc` is Fed Policy working; a surge in `ai capex cycle` is a miss. **The trends board** (`NarrativeTrends`, mounted on `/`) plots it under [ADR-0126](docs/adrs/0126-a-chart-without-a-value-axis-is-a-shape.md) — value axis with tick labels, every line direct-labelled with a **leader line** where the anti-collision pass displaces it, and a figures table. Every value existed only inside a `<title>` tooltip, which is **hover-only and absent on touch**, so it can never be the only copy. **Two clipped marks, one root cause: centring a mark on a coordinate that IS the plot boundary.** The waterfall used `slot = plotWidth/(n+1)` and placed the total at `slot × (n+1)` — the right plot edge exactly — measured in the live DOM at **683.5 → 724.5 against a 720 viewBox**. That second one *I did not find by reading* — I wrote the viewBox-overflow assertion for the waterfall and it failed on the Monte Carlo test I had just written to pass. Bars now derive from **bin edges** and clamp to the plot; the total takes a slot of its own; the regression test **sweeps book sizes 2/3/5/9/14**, because the original was invisible at the size the unit test happened to use. **The colour was carrying a meaning the app gives it elsewhere.** Ran the dataviz palette validator rather than eyeballing: `--long` (#126e53) against `--short` (#9f172a) measures **ΔE 6.1 under deuteranopia** — inside the 6–8 band that is legal *only* with secondary encoding. The scatter had put the **sign of risk** on colour while **direction** — what colour means on every other surface in this app — rode on a **radius difference of 1px** (4 vs 5) with no legend; and since every contribution is currently positive, all nine dots rendered in one colour and the channel carried nothing. Colour now means direction, **long is a filled dot and short a ring**, risk sign moved to the y-axis, and every other colour-coded sign got a printed value or a baseline behind it. **The fan is an emphasis chart, not three hues** — it had drawn p90 and p99 in the *same token* at different opacity, so the two pink lines were genuinely unidentifiable. p95 is now the accent, p90/p99 the de-emphasis grey, all three **direct-labelled at their right ends**. Its legend also stopped calling p95 a horizon: **p95 is a confidence level; 21d is the scored horizon** ([ADR-0090](docs/adrs/0090-a-published-pick-must-be-falsifiable.md)) — a category error sitting on the one figure `pick_outcomes` grades against. **And two counts printed side by side were reading different denominators**: `CorrelationMatrix` showed `{matrixPairs.length} measured · {sameDirection} same-direction` with `sameDirection` counted over the *flagged* subset — latent today, but it renders as **"36 measured · 0 same-direction"** the moment the matrix persists, meaning *"0 of 0 cleared the flag"*. **Verified against an isolated production build, not the dev server** — both of the shared tree's `next dev` instances were mid-rebuild and I twice audited *another session's* server before noticing `EADDRINUSE` on 3100 and 3101. All five charts now report **zero viewBox overflow** in the live DOM, the waterfall prints **8.05%**, the scatter's axes read 0.0%–2.0% and 0.0ז30.0×, zero console errors. 648 frontend tests / 53 files, tsc clean. Captures `docs/captures/2026-07-27/chart-*.png`. **Pipeline:** snapshotted all four book tables first, probed MiniMax before spending (it answered **529 overloaded**, so I polled to a 200 rather than discovering it mid-L5). The model had answered a numeric field with an explanation, which is a thing models do. `_num()` already existed in that module and **its docstring already said it exists because a non-numeric value "takes down the whole L5 run"** — the sizing path simply never used it. | 2026-07-27 | **ADR-0115 said "cap the complex and let the covariance split it". Measured it; it does not split — and widening the menu, the obvious next step, makes it worse.** [ADR-0116](docs/adrs/0116-the-optimizer-chooses-the-instrument-the-thesis-argues-the-idea.md). mu is `sign x |EdgeScore| x sigma` (ADR-0111), and inside a complex the covariance is near-singular by construction (rho 0.989, condition number 322), so the LINEAR term decides and the linear term is EdgeScore. Measured on three names: equal mu splits **33/33/33**, a **5% mu difference funds one name at 100%**. So ADR-0115's cap bounded the exposure while the instrument CHOICE stayed `max(|EdgeScore|)` — the same criterion `strongest` used, relocated into the solver where it is invisible. It had removed a choice it only hid. Handing the optimizer the whole menu was then measured too: **five names ranked by mu still funds SVXY at 100%** — a wider field for one weak signal, Michaud's estimation-error maximisation one level down. **The fix is two changes that only work together**, and `test_widening_alone_still_corners` exists so that finding cannot be re-forgotten. (1) `equalise_within_complexes` gives one complex one mu, so the objective's linear term depends only on the complex TOTAL — which the cap already bounds — and the split falls entirely to the quadratic term: the book carries the exposure through its least-variance member. **SVXY 55% vol goes to 0% and SPY 15% carries the bet**, with no hardcoded table of which instruments are sound — the outcome ADR-0115 wanted and explicitly declined to encode. Kept OUT of `build_mu`, which stays Grinold-Kahn and nothing else. (2) `_complex_expressions` offers the optimizer every same-side pool member of a complex a pick bought into; a complex no pick bought into stays out, so this widens HOW an idea is expressed and never WHICH ideas are held. **"Basket" was the wrong model and ADR-0115 used it**: with unequal vols the split is 75/25, i.e. minimum variance, not even — at rho 0.99 the diversification benefit is negligible and the vol saving large, so *cheapest clean expression* is the right description. **Valid only while gross is SLACK** (live book ~58% of budget): a levered member is capital-efficient when gross binds, and a common mu would then wrongly penalise it — stated in the ADR rather than left to be discovered. **The thesis had to move to the idea** or a substituted instrument is orphaned: L5 now writes an `exposure` per pick, the prompt tells it the sizer may hold the idea through a correlated ticker and that a case depending on one instrument's specifics must say so. A funded expression gets a deterministic sentence naming where its argument lives — it does **not** restate the parent's prose (a paragraph arguing SVXY on a row holding SPY is the precise failure this removes) and inherits **no citations**, because `verify_citations` adjudicated those numerals against text this row did not write. **Two defects fixed on the way**: the optimizer warned `no correlation complex mapping` once per standalone name, burying the real taxonomy gaps the sector/geo warnings exist to surface; and `binding_constraints` printed `long::0`, an internal index naming nothing a reader can act on — `complex_labels` now carries L5's `exposure` through, and the book, the constraints and `/ask` all use one label. Reached `/ask` and MCP too, per ADR-0100. **The two sizers now differ in MEMBERSHIP, not just weights** — the conviction sizer has no covariance so a wider menu would make it CONCENTRATE the exposure; it holds exactly L5's names, and `optimizer_result.complex_expression` says so rather than leaving ADR-0053's lesson to be relearned. No migration: `picks` is JSONB. **963 backend tests** (from 927), 628 frontend, tsc clean. The load-bearing one is `test_removing_the_handoff_loses_the_expression`, which severs the wiring and requires the book to change — the failure class that produced a 0%-gross book from a green optimizer once already. **Open:** `independent_ideas.strongest` and `fallback_picks._diversify` still select on EdgeScore; substitutability at rho 0.70 is now a load-bearing assumption; the published book shows pre-fix values until the next nightly run. |

| 2026-07-27 | **Bar 1: sizing takes four inputs now, and the fourth is stated rather than filled.** `GOAL.md`'s first bar from the practitioner transcript. Vol and conviction already entered through μ this morning; this wires the third and answers the fourth honestly. **Crowding enters as a per-name CAP, not a μ haircut** ([ADR-0110](docs/adrs/0110-crowding-caps-what-it-can-see.md)). Two reasons, both load-bearing. *Semantics:* crowding says the exit is narrow — a liquidity fact that supports holding **less**, not a claim that the position will **return** less, which nothing has measured. *Auditability:* a cap lands in `binding_constraints` naming the position it bit, and names itself as **tightened** rather than reporting as the standing 20%; a μ adjustment would leave a reader looking at a smaller weight with no way to attribute it — which fails the bar's own test, since that test is about whether a reader can **see** why. **This reverses an accepted ADR and needed a superseding one, not an edit.** ADR-0097's Decision says verbatim *"Nothing here recommends a trade, reverses one, or feeds sizing"*, and that sentence lived in three places that had to move together — the module docstring, the ADR, and `/risk`'s footnote. ADR-0110 supersedes **that clause and only that clause**; coverage-first, the inverse flip, `fetched=False` as distinct, and miners-are-not-the-metal all stand unchanged. **The constraint is the hard part, and "degrade to neutral" is pinned bit-for-bit.** `crowding_caps` returns a map containing **only** the names it tightens, so an unmapped name keeps the base cap and `test_crowding_is_neutral_where_unobservable` asserts its weight is **identical** — not close — with the input wired and unwired. **The bias is named, not designed away:** penalising only what we can see gives a structural advantage to what we cannot, so over many runs the book drifts toward instruments nobody can observe. A coverage floor would remove it by refusing the signal, which on today's 22% coverage means it never binds — **failing bar 1's own test** and trading a real bias for a fake neutrality. So `coverage_share` is persisted every run and the drift is watchable instead. **Both sizers apply the same map**, computed once — a crowding input the optimizer honoured and the fallback ignored would be ADR-0053 wearing a different field name — and a per-name entry may only ever **tighten**: `_cap_vector` and `allocate_portfolio` both take `min(base, entry)`, so a COT print cannot loosen this book's published risk policy. **The COT fetch moved ahead of sizing.** It ran at persist time; two fetches in one run can return two different readings, leaving the book **sized** by one and **explained** by the other — the exact defect ADR-0099 had to restructure the chokepoint signal to avoid. One read in `aggregate_context` over the candidate pool (picks are a subset), reused by `_positioning_row`, with a **sentinel** distinguishing "never set" from "fetched and got `{}`" — an empty dict is a real finding and must not trigger a refetch. `test_the_reading_that_sizes_is_the_reading_that_is_persisted` monkeypatches the fetcher to **raise** and asserts it is never called. **Measured on the live 2026-07-25 book: nothing was tightened, and that is the result.** Coverage 22.2% — only SHY and SVXY map to a contract — and both sit mid-range (COT index 69.9 and 33.2) against the 80/20 threshold, so the recorded reason is *"2 observable positions were checked and none sits at a speculator extreme"*, distinct from both "we did not look" and "nothing maps". The other eight carry `cause: "no_contract"`. The machinery is wired, tested and correct and today changes no weight because the data says nothing — **not a reason to loosen the multiplier**, which stays a named judgement (`CROWDED_CAP_MULTIPLIER = 0.5`) with the same standing as the 80/20 cut. **`unobservable` records gain a structural `cause`** (`no_contract` / `not_retrieved` / `insufficient_history`) beside the prose reason, because sizing has to tell "COT will never cover this" from "COT covers it and we failed to read it today", and parsing the rendered sentence would make the copy load-bearing. **The fourth input is answered with a documented absence** ([ADR-0111](docs/adrs/0111-mu-is-the-return-input-and-what-it-is-not.md)). `optimizer_result.mu` is the only per-name number denominated in expected return — but its cross-sectional content is `sign × \|EdgeScore\| × σ`, a rank converted into return units carrying no view of how far a name can travel. Three proxies were considered and rejected: an LLM-emitted target (the guardrail exists because model numbers cannot be trusted, and there is no source to check it against), the S5 melt-up shocks (authored constants, not measurements — sizing on them would turn a stress calibration into a return forecast), and `pct_from_ma` (which measures distance-to-**disqualifier** and is backwards for this). A proxy that makes a gap look closed is worse than the gap. GOAL.md bar 1 is updated to say three-of-four settled, the fourth addressed in units and absent in substance, and the claim that `expected_return` appears nowhere is marked **stale**. **883 backend tests** (from 872), **584 frontend**, tsc clean, verified under the real entrypoint in a subprocess. `/book`'s Sizing panel states coverage whether or not anything was tightened — "nothing was crowded" and "almost nothing could be checked" are the two readings a reader must be able to tell apart — and `/risk`'s footnote, which said the opposite, is rewritten. |

So the happy path is two calls and a rejected first answer costs **three**. That matters because the cap exists to protect tomorrow's book, and it turns out to bound the thing it protects only within a 1.5x band. | 2026-07-27 | **`Ask` became a persistent floating window too — and the reason is sharper than the TV's.** Owner: *"should Ask stay a floating card/tab too, so I can click elsewhere and go to a different page without losing it? should it anchor bottom right?"* Yes on both, and the argument is not symmetry with `Live news`: a dropped broadcast costs nothing and resumes, whereas a dropped transcript is **gone for good and was expensive**. `AskConsole` deliberately keeps no history (ADR-0087 — nothing here writes to the domain database) and every turn costs **two LLM calls on the same MiniMax quota the nightly book spends**, so the ordinary act of reading an answer and then clicking through to `/book` was destroying something that had been paid for. `keepMounted` encodes exactly that asymmetry in one flag: `Ask` HIDES on close (transcript survives; a page reload still clears it, which is the boundary ADR-0087 cares about), `Live news` UNMOUNTS (a collapsed tab makes no request to Google — ADR-0104's privacy property, kept). One switch, "expensive to rebuild, cheap to keep" versus "cheap to rebuild, expensive to keep". **`FloatingWindow` was extracted** for drag/collapse/clamp/portal/persist, and **`DockPopover` was DELETED rather than kept**: I extracted it two changes ago for two consumers, `Live news` left, now `Ask` has left, and an abstraction with no consumers is dead scaffolding. Corners at the owner's direction: `Ask` bottom-right (chat-widget convention, reading column clear), `Live news` top-right. **Then the measurement contradicted the premise of that choice, and it is worth recording rather than quietly absorbing.** Opposite corners only separate the two if both are short: expanded, the TV is **524px** and Ask **437px**, so they need ~960px of vertical room against the ~780px a 1440×900 laptop leaves after the header and status ribbon. Measured — **1440×1080: 9px gap, clean. 1440×900: 171px overlap. 1280×800: 271px overlap** — and at 900 the TV covered Ask's *entire* title bar, so its close and collapse buttons were unreachable: a window you can see and cannot dismiss, which is goal 8's trap arrived at from the other side. **Click-to-front fixes it** (any pointer touch raises a window; the recovery path is to click the buried window's visible half, which is what a reader would do anyway). **That fix was itself broken on the first attempt, and only measuring caught it**: the guard `current === zCounter ? current : ++zCounter` is a permanent no-op, because both windows START at the counter's value, so the guard is true immediately, the counter never advances, and both sat at the same z with DOM order deciding — precisely what the feature exists to override. Windows now start at 0 ("never raised") and the counter is bumped outside the state updater, since React double-invokes updaters under StrictMode. **17 browser assertions pass**: both corners correct; Ask survives an outside click, survives navigating `/` → `/book` with its transcript intact, and **the transcript survives close and reopen**; click-to-front raises Ask above the TV (z 63 > 62); the TV still survives navigation with its player mounted and still unmounts it on collapse; drag works and persists; at 375 both open collapsed, on screen, no overflow. Zero console errors, 568 tests / 48 files green, tsc clean. **The transcript test STUBS `/api/chat` via route interception and spent zero real quota** — the thing under test is React state retention, not the agent, and an exhausted quota does not produce a broken chat, it produces tomorrow's book as a template with no thesis. **Known and deliberate:** at ≤1000px viewport height the two windows overlap at their defaults. Click-to-front makes that navigable and either can be collapsed or dragged, but it is a real cost of the chosen corners and is not hidden here. Captures `docs/captures/2026-07-27/two-windows-1440x{900,1080}.png` show both cases. |
| 2026-07-27 | **The book is sized by an optimizer now, and the quant layer it needed was read across from im-Jarvis.** Owner asked whether im-Jarvis's deterministic calculations and portfolio optimization were relevant here. They are: Andromeda had **no optimizer at all** — a grep for `cvxpy\|scipy.optimize\|mean_variance\|efficient_frontier\|risk_parity\|black_litterman\|kelly` across `backend/`, `scripts/` and `tests/` returned zero hits, sizing was `conviction = \|EdgeScore\|/vol` clamped by a fixed-point loop, and the covariance matrix computed on every run was pure reporting that never touched a weight. **10 modules ported and wired, 4 skipped as duplicates, 22 named as not-ported with the specific data each would need** ([ADR-0109](docs/adrs/0109-what-was-read-across-from-im-jarvis-and-what-was-not.md)) — no CUSIPs so no bond maths, no private assets, no trade blotter, no orders; porting those would have manufactured exactly the dead-but-plausible code ADR-0099/0101 were written about. **The optimizer is a reimplementation, not a lift, and the reason is a live defect in the source**: `_quantise_weights` does `clipped = [max(x, 0.0) for x in raw]` — unconditionally, *not* gated on its own `long_only` flag — then renormalises to sum 1. On a long-short book that **deletes every short and returns a fully-invested long-only book that looks structurally perfect**. `sum(w) == 1` also contradicts ADR-0037, and for a long-short book it constrains the NET, so a book could satisfy it at 300% gross. So: signs survive, `gross ≤ 1` keeps the cash, the 20/30/35 caps become **solver constraints binding on the group total** rather than post-hoc clamps, and direction is pinned by `s_i·w_i ≥ 0` — which also keeps the problem linear, since a fixed sign makes the magnitude an affine expression instead of `\|w\|`. `risk_parity` and `black_litterman` were deliberately NOT ported: the former's `log(w)` barrier is structurally long-only, the latter needs market-cap weights we have no source for, and a stub that silently degrades is worse than an absence. **The missing piece was μ, and it had to be designed rather than translated** ([ADR-0108](docs/adrs/0108-expected-returns-are-constructed-not-assumed.md)): EdgeScore is a ranking signal in [−1,1], and handing it to an optimizer as an expected return is a units error no test on the optimizer could catch, because the optimizer would be solving its problem correctly. Grinold–Kahn `μ = IC × σ × z`, IC read from `backtest_results` and **never defaulted**, blend weights renormalised over the 3 of 5 components actually testable (trend +0.033 n=975, carry +0.127 n=94, value +0.094 n=94; regime and sentiment are not), shrunk 50% per ADR-0033 → **+0.0745 raw becomes +0.0373**. z is **scaled, not demeaned** — L5 already chose the sides, and demeaning would hand a below-average long a negative μ. A non-positive composite yields no μ at all rather than a flipped one. **Measured on the published 2026-07-25 book, all 10 names priced: gross 59.3%→56.1%, net −5.4%→+1.6%, ex-ante vol 8.39%→7.44%, expected return +0.675%→+0.728%, return/vol 0.080→0.098** — more return at less risk inside the same limits, with the geography cap binding at the solution and 43.9% in cash. Rebalancing costs 36.3% turnover / $54,412. **Two results that deserve suspicion rather than a victory lap**: SHY is zeroed (at 0.09% daily vol its μ is +0.04%, so the optimizer declines to spend gross on it), and **SVXY quadruples from 3.94% to 16.07%** — inside the 20% cap, but that is mean-variance error-maximisation concentrating into an inverse-VIX ETF whose tail is precisely what a covariance estimated on 252 calm days understates. Watch it. **A real bug, caught by the wiring test and by nothing else**: the first implementation passed `state["picks"]` to `build_mu`, and those are the model's own dicts which have never carried `edge_score` — so every μ came back 0.0 and the optimizer correctly deployed **nothing**, a 0%-gross book out of a green solve. That is ADR-0053's seam one layer along. The sixteen unit tests in `test_optimizer.py` all build their inputs by hand and could not have caught it; `test_optimizer_wiring.py` asserts the path end to end and did. Same file also pins that `sizing_method` reaches the persisted row, which no per-component test can. **Also landed**: Student-t Monte Carlo VaR (seeded, so ADR-0013's determinism holds), the √t VaR fan whose **21-day** point finally matches the horizon ADR-0090 actually scores picks over, EWMA + GARCH(1,1) conditional vol, benchmark comparison (TE / IR / **up-down capture**, the one that tests this book's own claim to be short the market), a linear transaction-cost model, and historical VaR/ES + Sortino + Calmar + max drawdown beside the parametric ones on `risk_engine`. **Every added risk metric is a separate column with its own method id** — there are now three VaR numbers on this book and ADR-0082 already had to say once that two unlabelled ones is a regression. `volatility_models` was about to ship with no caller, which ADR-0099 says is not implemented; it is wired into `compute_and_persist_risk` as reporting-only `conditional_vol`, explicitly NOT into the conviction denominator, because swapping that changes every published weight and is a separate decision. **`ARCHITECTURE.md`'s Cap Enforcement section was already lying** — it documented a `≥ 3 members` guard and a final `normalize once` step, both of which ADR-0037 removed (2 × 20% = 40% > 30% disproves the first; the second undid every cap above it). Rewritten. **872 tests green, up from 790.** cvxpy added to `requirements.txt`, `ci.yml` **and** `daily-refresh.yml` — the last two pip-install explicit lists, and missing the third does not fail the run, it silently reverts sizing to conviction.
