| 2026-07-27 | **`Live news` became a persistent floating window — because as a popover it was being killed by the exact interaction it existed for.** Owner: *"it should stay a floating card/tab so I can click elsewhere and go to a different page without losing the live news TV."* The diagnosis is one sentence: a popover dismisses on any outside `pointerdown`, **and every nav link is an outside pointerdown**, so "leave it running and go read `/book`" was the one thing that closed it. That also settles a question left open yesterday, when `Ask` and `Live news` were unified under one `DockPopover` — they should never have shared dismissal. A question is something you finish; a broadcast's entire value is *continuing while you do something else*. **So the abstraction split along the seam that actually differs**: `RibbonControl` now holds the shared LOOK (label gated to `wide`, `aria-label` always) so the two still read as siblings in the ribbon, `DockPopover` keeps dismiss-on-outside-click for `Ask` alone, and `LiveNewsWindow` is a persistent window that closes only on its own button. `DockPopover` is down to one consumer and is kept as a component anyway — what it encodes (Escape restores focus to the trigger, `pointerdown` before the target's own handler, viewport-anchoring below `sm`) is easy to get subtly wrong and invisible on a desktop screenshot. **Two implementation facts that are not decoration.** It **portals to `document.body`**: TopBar carries `backdrop-blur-md`, and an element with a `backdrop-filter` becomes the CONTAINING BLOCK for `position: fixed` descendants — rendered inside the header, a "fixed" window is trapped in a 56px bar. And it **survives navigation for free**: `TopBar` is rendered by `app/layout.tsx`, which the App Router does not remount on a client-side route change, so the state and the playing iframe persist with no global store (a hard reload still resets it — that is a page load, not a navigation). Draggable from the title bar with pointer capture, clamped into the viewport on drag, collapse and resize, position persisted to `localStorage`. **ADR-0104's privacy guarantee is preserved and slightly STRENGTHENED**: the player is mounted only while EXPANDED, so a collapsed tab is as quiet as a closed panel — measured, **0 requests to youtube/ytimg before opening**, and the iframe is genuinely UNMOUNTED when collapsed rather than `display:none`, which would have left a live third-party frame running behind a hidden box. Phones open **collapsed by default** (owner's choice), so the mobile default is also the private one. **Two real bugs, both caught by measuring rather than by looking.** (1) The collapse preference never took: an effect persisted `collapsed` on every change, so it fired on mount with the initial `false` and **overwrote the stored value before the open handler read it** — "no preference yet" was unreachable and the phone default could never fire. The read now happens once on mount into a ref and the write happens only in the toggle. (2) At the default corner the window sat **on** the fixed `LiveFeed` status ribbon at both 1440 and 375; the inset is now read from the `--feed-h` custom property rather than hardcoded, for the reason `globals.css` gives where it declares it. **17 browser assertions, all passing**: 0 third-party requests before open; window portalled to `<body>` and not inside the header; player mounted when expanded; **survives an outside click**; **survives navigating `/` → `/book` with the iframe still mounted**; drags (988,335)→(668,163) and persists the position; stays fully on screen; collapse leaves the window but unmounts the iframe; closes on its own button; **`Ask` still dismisses on outside click, unchanged**; and at 375 it opens collapsed, on screen, with no page overflow. Zero console errors. 568 tests / 48 files green, tsc clean. Verified on an isolated server (`NEXT_DIST_DIR=.next-mine`, port 3200) since `:3000` is the concurrent session's; `tsconfig.json` auto-edit reverted and `.next-mine` removed. **Docs**: `design-goals.md` now **states the widening plainly** rather than burying it — the real-time exception used to end when you left `/`, and a live picture can now follow a reader across every page; what bounds it (no derived data, nothing traceable to it, `/ask` cannot quote it, the cadence caveat travels inside the window) is unchanged and still enforced. `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `CLAUDE.md` updated to match. ADR-0104 is untouched: what it decided still holds. Captures `docs/captures/2026-07-27/live-news-window-{on-book,collapsed,375}.png`. |

| 2026-07-27 | **`Live news` moved to the ribbon beside `Ask` — and the move surfaced a header that never fitted.** Owner's direction. `LiveNewsDock` left `/`'s page header for `TopBar`, so it is now available on **every page** rather than one; it sits beside `Ask` because both are ways of READING the site rather than things it publishes, which is the same argument that keeps `Ask` out of the four-item nav. It resolves through `resolveRunDates`, the same pure function `/` uses, so the two cannot drift; verified the caveat renders **2026-07-25** on both `/` and `/book`. **Then the measurements said I had made something worse.** At 375 the panel, which anchored `absolute right-0` to a control that used to be the rightmost thing on its row, now had the run-state group to its right — so a 345px panel opened at **left = -88** on a 375px screen. Fixed with viewport-anchored insets below `sm`, unchanged button-anchoring above. **The header itself was the real find, and it was already broken.** The nav was `justify-self-center`, which sizes a grid item to its CONTENT and centres it — so `min-w-0` was **inert**, the four items could never shrink, and they simply painted outward over the logo and the tool cluster. **Measured on the deployed, pre-change build: `Method` ended at 237 against a cluster starting at 210 — a 27px overlap at 375, and 3px at 640.** Adding a 34px control took it to **48px, with `Themes` pushed to -14**, off the left edge. Three fixes, each from a measurement: the nav now fills its track and contains its items (`overflow-x-auto scrollbar-none`) — **not** `justify-center`, because centred flex content that overflows spills past the START edge where no scroll can reach it, so the items carry `first:ml-auto last:mr-auto`, which centres the row when it fits and collapses to left-aligned-and-scrollable when it does not; nav padding holds at `px-2.5` until `md`; and the run-state **timestamp moved from `sm` to `lg`** — at 640 that group was **159px of a 269px cluster** while the nav was **49px short**, so the most redundant thing on the row (the word beside it already says live or stale) was the one paying for it. **Result: all four nav items fit with nothing clipped and nothing overlapping from 640 up — better than the pre-change build, which collided at 640.** At 375–480 the nav still cannot show all four, but it never could; it is now contained and scrollable instead of colliding, which is the honest version of the same constraint. The label is gated to `wide` (1424px) — **exactly** where the nav goes `wide:hidden` and hands over to the SideRail, so the control spells itself out only once it is not competing for the row; `aria-label` carries the name whenever the text is hidden. **A verification finding worth more than the change:** `/book`, `/risk` and `/method` were completely un-hydrated on the shared `:3000` dev server — TopBar's effects never ran, its own `Data live/stale` cell read `—`, and **no** client control on those pages responded. Had I trusted `:3000` I would have "found" a hydration bug I did not cause and chased it. 568 tests / 48 files green, tsc clean, zero console errors, no page overflow at any width tested. `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `design-goals.md` both described this as "a panel on `/`" and now say otherwise; ADR-0104 is left alone, since what it actually bought — closed by default, no third-party request until opened, caveat outside the open/closed branch, a control and not a fifth destination — is unchanged by the move. | 2026-07-27 | **A layout audit of `/` — the pane grid was 29% empty, and the spacing bugs were all relics of a lock that had already been removed.** Asked to check card arrangement, grid and spacing, so I measured the rendered boxes rather than reading the classes. **The grid was the real defect, and it was invisible in the source.** `lg:grid-cols-3` held four panes with `themes` at `lg:col-span-2 lg:row-span-2`; auto-flow then put `discovery` at **row 3, column 1**, leaving columns 2–3 of that row empty — an **891×731px hole** at 1440. The row span compounded it: `items-start` means a pane never stretches, so `themes` (1450px) never filled the **1868px its span reserved**, parking a **435px void** directly under it (**576px at 1024**, where the disparity is worse). Nothing spans rows now, so no pane reserves height it cannot fill. Grid **2615px → 2128px**, dead space **29% → 9.6%**, document **3211 → 2748px** at 1440 (**3683 → 3043** at 1024). `Discovered themes` also got *shorter* (731 → 480px) purely from being twice as wide. **The three spacing bugs shared one cause: `lg:mb-0` left behind when ADR-0103's viewport lock was removed.** Under the lock `main` was a flex column supplying `gap`, so children zeroed their own margins; the lock went (the comment at `page.tsx:408` says so) and the overrides stayed. Measured top-level gaps at 1440 read **0, 24, 16, 16, 0** — the page header sat *flush* on the MarketBar and the screening strip *flush* on the heatmap card, **at desktop only; mobile was correct throughout**, which is exactly why it survived review. `MarketBar`'s root carried `mb-6`, the only 24 in a 16 rhythm. `RegimeHero` carried `mb-6 lg:mb-0` while its caller already wraps it in `mb-4`: at lg the two resolved to 16, but below it the child's 24 **collapsed through the wrapper and won**, making mobile the one breakpoint where the block sat 8px low. All three now let the caller own the gap. Rhythm is a uniform **16px at 1440, 1024 and 375**. **`TerminalPane`'s own `mb-4 lg:mb-0` was left alone** — there the grid's `lg:gap-4` genuinely does replace it, which is the distinction that made the other three wrong. **Then the owner looked at the result and named the remaining defect precisely: "the prediction market card height can be taller? to align with both 'theme momentum' and 'discovered themes'."** Measured, that was two misalignments from one cause. `crowd` was pinned to `lg:row-start-2`, so it inherited a top edge set by `discovery` in **columns 1-2** — a neighbour it does not touch. It therefore began at **1466**, a full **275px below where `headlines` ended (1191)**, and ran to **2128**, **182px past** `discovery`'s bottom at 1946. The column was being measured against the wrong thing at both ends. **Fixed by making column 3 one rail** (`lg:row-span-2 lg:self-stretch`, a flex column) instead of two independently-placed cells: row heights are then set by columns 1-2 alone, the rail stretches across both, and `crowd` takes the remainder with `flex-1`. Prediction markets **662 → 740px**, top at **1207** — 27px under `headlines`, level with the `Theme momentum` card across the page — and bottom at **1946, exactly `discovery`'s**. `PredictionMarkets` gained `lg:h-full` + a flex column and its tile grid `lg:flex-1 lg:auto-rows-fr`, so the extra height **spreads across the tile rows** rather than pooling into a dead band under the last tile; it also squares off rows that differed only because one question wrapped to two lines. **A 15px residual at 1024 was caught by measuring rather than by eye** and is the interesting half: where the rail's content needs more room than columns 1-2 supply, the *row* grows to fit the rail (559 against `discovery`'s 545), and under `items-start` the pane keeps its content height — so the misalignment reappeared, 15px smaller, at one breakpoint. `discovery` now carries `lg:self-stretch` and `DiscoveredThemes` `lg:h-full`; stretching the pane without the card would have moved the ragged edge inward instead of removing it. **Bottoms are now exact at 1440 and 1024** (1946 and 2138, asserted equal, not eyeballed). Grid **2128 → 1946px**, document **2748 → 2566px** at 1440. **The owner then found the flaw in that fix, and was right about the causality**: *"the news card's bottom should align with the 'Top 3 themes by attention' section — it's overshooting and causing the prediction market card's top edge to be unable to align with the theme momentum top edge."* The rail aligned the **bottom** of column 3 but left its **internal split** wherever the feed's last headline happened to fall. **The root cause is that the split the reader sees on the left was never a grid line**: `Theme momentum` lived *inside* the scores pane, separated from the conviction cards by a collapsed `mb-8`/`mt-6` margin, so **nothing in column 3 could align to it** — the feed ran 43px past the cards (1191 vs 1148) and `crowd` inherited whatever edge that produced (1207 against momentum's 1180). No arithmetic in column 3 could fix a boundary that did not exist as a row. **`Theme momentum` is now its own grid row**, giving three rows — `heatmap + top 3 | headlines`, `momentum | crowd (spans rows 2-3)`, `discovered themes |` — so the three pairings hold **by construction**: the feed ends on the row-1 line, `crowd` starts on the row-2 line, and `crowd` runs to the bottom of `discovered themes`. **All three measured EXACT at 1440 (1191 / 1207 / 1973) and at 1024 (1288 / 1304 / 2138)**, asserted as equalities rather than eyeballed. Four panes opt out of `items-start` with `self-stretch`, and **each one's card carries `lg:h-full` or a `lg:flex-1` absorber to match** — the top-3 row is the scores pane's absorber, so the conviction cards grow into the slack (+43px) instead of the pane ending early; stretching a pane without stretching its card just moves the ragged edge inward, the trap this page fell into twice. **Moving the block is licensed, not a reversal**: it lived inside the scores pane under ADR-0103's lock (*"the pane scrolls; the document does not"*), and **the lock is gone** — what the 2026-07-27 restoration directive required was that the block not be **dropped**, and nothing required it to be a child of that pane. It keeps its own hash target (`#momentum`), consistent with ADR-0103 constraint 3. Grid **1946 → 1973px**, document **2566 → 2593px** — this fix *costs* 27px, which is the correct trade for an edge that holds at any content length. **Mobile re-checked because a DOM block moved**: order is still `themes → momentum → headlines → crowd → discovery`, no horizontal scroll at 375, zero console errors, and the page got 40px *shorter* (7025 → 6985) as the dropped `mb-8` handed spacing to the uniform 16px gap. **Verified by measuring the live DOM at three widths before and after, not by screenshot alone**; **375 is byte-identical at 7025px through every step** — all of this is `lg:`-gated and mobile was correct from the start. 568 tests / 48 files green, tsc clean. Captures `docs/captures/2026-07-27/home-layout-audit-{1440,1024,375}.png`. **Environment note:** Playwright MCP was held by the concurrent session, so this ran through a scratchpad node script resolving `playwright` out of `frontend/node_modules` via `createRequire` — the MCP being busy is not a blocker. |

| 2026-07-26 | **The regime model's breadth input was a boolean wearing a percentage — and because of where the thresholds sat, it was silently deciding the sentiment label.** [ADR-0091](docs/adrs/0091-breadth-must-be-a-share-of-something-named.md). `_compute_spx_breadth` asked one question — is SPY above its own 200d MA — and returned **65.0 for yes, 35.0 for no, nothing between**. What made it a correctness problem is `_classify_sentiment`, whose breadth rules are `< 40 → risk-off` and `> 60 → risk-on`: **35 and 65 sit either side of both thresholds, so whenever breadth resolved it fired one of them**. The VIX, term-structure and credit rules above it only fire in genuine extremes (VIX > 25, HY > 500bp), so the function's own third outcome, `neutral`, was **unreachable on any day the price feed worked** — a four-input sentiment model that was in practice a one-bit switch on SPY. The freshly backfilled year was the evidence: 244 risk-on, 20 risk-off, **zero neutral**, tracking SPY-vs-MA almost exactly. **Now a real share of a named universe**: the eleven GICS sector SPDRs above their own 200d MA, 0–100. **Not the 500 constituents**, deliberately — there is no constituent list in the repo, and using *today's* membership to measure a 2025 date imports survivorship bias into a historical series, i.e. measuring the index that exists now, backwards, and calling it history. The eleven ETFs existed on every date in the range, and they partition the index by construction. The cost is 9.1-point granularity, disclosed rather than smoothed. A **moving denominator is refused** (fewer than 8 members with a full window → `None`), because a share computed over whichever tickers happened to download is not comparable to yesterday's. The UI label was corrected in the same change: a source line describing a different computation than the one that ran is worse than no source line. **Recomputed 264 rows — and stopped at 2026-07-21 on purpose.** Books published 07-22 → 07-25 **cite breadth** (07-24's `book_view`, its picks and its citations all reference it), so overwriting those regime rows would leave a published thesis citing "65" against a row now reading 81.82 — the silent history-rewrite this product exists to prevent. **The column therefore carries a documented discontinuity**: a real share up to 07-21, the old 65/35 flag for the four published days, the real share again from the next live run. Four legacy rows is the price of not retconning four published books. **Result: `neutral` appeared for the first time** (1 row), breadth now takes 7 distinct values from 45.45 to 100.00, and **9 rows land between the two thresholds** — days the VIX and credit rules now decide instead of being overruled by a boolean. 34 regime tests pass, tsc and lint clean. |
| 2026-07-26 | **Backfilled a year of L3 regime history — and the backfill's first act was to expose a look-ahead leak in the classifier it was reusing.** `macro_daily_history` held ~250 trading days per series while `regime_classifications` held **four rows**, and L3 is a pure function of exactly those observations, so a year of history was already in the database in all but name. **The leak:** every input `classify()` reads was already bounded by `as_of` — someone had built that deliberately, with a comment naming look-ahead bias — **except `_compute_spx_breadth()`, which took no date at all** and always read SPY's latest close. Backfilling with it would have stamped *today's* breadth onto 250 historical rows, and breadth is the one input capable of flipping the sentiment label. Invisible while the only caller was "classify right now"; wrong 250 times the moment it wasn't. Now takes `as_of` (and an injectable `hist`, so the property is testable without a network), with the SPY download cached once per process instead of 250 identical round trips. **Also recorded, not fixed:** despite its name and the `%` the UI gives it, breadth returns **65.0 or 35.0 and nothing between** — a binary SPY-above-its-MA flag wearing a percentage, not the share of index members above their own MA. That is why `/ask` said "S&P 500 breadth of 65.00%". Changing what the number MEANS is a separate decision from fixing when it is measured. **`scripts/backfill_regime.py` drives `RegimeClassifier.classify(run_date)` rather than reimplementing the rules** — which matters because the classifier converts percent to basis points before applying thresholds calibrated in bp, a conversion whose absence once made two of three cycle rules unreachable; a private copy of that arithmetic is how the divergence comes back. Dry-run by default, `--apply` to write, and **existing rows are never overwritten without `--overwrite`** because they are the published record. **The dry run caught its own bug:** it reported "175 candidate dates" for a year of data and looked entirely plausible — PostgREST caps a response at **1000 rows regardless of `.limit()`**, so six series over a year silently returned the earliest 1000 and nothing said so. Paginated; the real count is 267. **Result: 268 rows spanning 2025-07-22 → 2026-07-25**, VIX 13.47–31.05, curve 0.27–0.74pp, HY OAS 2.63–3.46% — real variation, which is itself the evidence that no look-ahead stamping occurred, and sentiment tracks VIX (2026-04-01, VIX 24.5 → risk-off). **One more find:** the oldest surviving row, 2026-07-21, was **demo seed data from migration 007** — `yield_curve_slope 20.0` (impossible) and `hy_oas 320.0` in basis points where every live row stores percent (2.77). Its own migration comment said it would be overwritten by the next live run; it never was, because the pipeline stamps its own date. Recomputed from real macro data, and the table's ranges are now all plausible. **What was deliberately NOT backfilled:** L1 HypeScore, because `mention_count` and `sentiment` come from Brave News and Reddit and nothing can reconstruct how much attention a theme drew on 2025-11-14 — backfilling only the price-derived half would put a number that is not HypeScore in the column that says it is; and the L5 book, because re-running the agent over reconstructed inputs makes a *simulated* book, and writing it to `research_recommendations` would assert the system published on days it did not. **The honest consequence: `backtest_hype.py` reads `theme_signals_history`, i.e. exactly the attention half that cannot be reconstructed, so HypeScore IC validation cannot be accelerated by any backfill — it accrues forward, one day at a time.** 31 regime tests pass (+8). The 14 `test_theme_discovery` failures in the same run are `ModuleNotFoundError: gensim`, a missing optional dep, pre-existing and unrelated. Also filled in root `.env`'s `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, which was the literal placeholder `replace-with-anon-key-from-dashboard` — **not**, as I had reported earlier, a rotated or expired key. |

Fixed by moving the rule to `lib/risk/sampleAdequacy.ts`, keyed by `portfolio_risk` column so any consumer of that table can look it up. `risk_metrics` now **withholds and says so**: the shortfall is named in `absence` ("Sharpe (3 sessions of return history, needs 60)") and the model is told to say they are *not published at this sample size* and explicitly **NOT** that they are unavailable — silently dropping the facts would have had `/ask` report the book has no risk figures at all. **An unknown sample does not withhold**: a failed count returns `unjudged` and the figure is quoted, because turning a transient read error into a blackout of every risk number is the opposite failure and a worse one. | 2026-07-26 | **An absence must say which kind of absence it is** — design goal 2 ("absence is stated, never filled") had been honoured **case by case**, and every case re-argued from scratch in a docstring, because `NumericDerivation` had one field for all of it: `display_status="unavailable"` plus free text. That single status was carrying at least three different statements — *we tried to measure it and could not*, *the question does not apply to this subject*, and *we never looked* — which demand different things of a reader: "come back, retry" versus "stop waiting, nothing is coming, ever". Rendering them alike tells someone to wait for a number that does not exist. One week produced three instances in three separate modules: `share_of_gross` on a book with no gross (**not_applicable**, ADR-0066), a COT reading for GDX where no futures contract exists (**not_applicable**, ADR-0097), and a COT reading when the CFTC portal did not answer (**unknown**). Nothing in the type system knew the difference. Adds `epistemic` = `known`/`unknown`/`not_applicable` as a **second axis**, independent of presentation — a `stale` figure is *known* (we have last week's value, it is merely old) and an `estimated` one is *known* (uncertain in magnitude, not existence). It **defaults to `known`, which is safe only because validation rejects a known absence**: an author who forgets the field and has a real value is correct by default, while one who forgets it and has `None` gets an error naming both choices they skipped — the common case costs nothing and the dangerous case is impossible. Both absences require a reason; a present value may not claim to be unknown. Where a fallback must guess it lands on the **weaker** claim (`unknown`), because "come back" is harmless if nothing was coming whereas "stop waiting" has to be earned — so L4's "insufficient history" is classified **unknown, not not_applicable**: the statistic really is undefined on a two-observation series, but the shortfall is in the data rather than the question and resolves as history accrues. **Timestamp roles became types.** `Observed`/`Published`/`Retrieved`/`Effective` are frozen wrappers isinstance-checked at construction, so passing retrieval where observation belongs **raises** instead of reporting a five-day-old CFTC reading as two days old; freshness is defined against *observation* and `as_of` must equal it. `Effective` is deliberately exempt from ordering because a revision published today can be effective for last quarter, and `publication_lag_seconds` is **None, not 0**, when unrecorded. **The frontend mirror is now enforced rather than asserted:** `lib/derivations/numeric.ts` had always claimed to "mirror numeric.py field-for-field" in a comment, and the drift that invites fails silently in the worst way — the backend emits a field, the interface does not declare it, every read is `undefined`, and a provenance page renders it as "no data" rather than as an error. `derivation-parity.test.ts` parses both files, compares field sets and union members, includes a **non-vacuity check** (two empty lists compare equal, so a regex that stopped matching would turn the file green while checking nothing), and was negative-tested by deleting a field. `absenceCopy()` gives the two absences different words and `RiskMetricsGrid` renders it, so the distinction reaches a reader instead of living in a type. **I removed a validation rule of my own that could not fire:** "an unavailable derivation cannot be epistemically known" is unreachable in any state the other two rules permit, and it shadowed a more useful message with a vaguer one — a test now asserts both routes into that contradiction stay closed. **Scope stated rather than overclaimed:** this is the type, not the sweep. The overlay columns, the freshness verdict and the track record still express the distinction in prose and `null` — correct today, not yet checkable — and `AdvisoryDerivation` is untouched because whether the same three states fit a *qualitative* claim deserves its own decision. **491 frontend, 776 backend (14 pre-existing gensim), tsc exit 0, lint clean.** | `backend/derivations/numeric.py`, `backend/services/risk_engine.py`, `frontend/lib/derivations/numeric.ts`, `frontend/components/risk/RiskMetricsGrid.tsx`, ADR-0098 |
| 2026-07-26 | **External positioning can see a fifth of the book, and that is the finding** — every crowding measure in the system was **internal**: HypeScore counts attention in a corpus we assemble, and `AttentionCrowding` ranks our own themes against each other, so neither could answer the question a reader actually has about a position — *is everyone else already in this trade?* CFTC **Commitments of Traders** answers it, and unlike the two worldmonitor feeds gated at $39.99/mo it is **free, official and keyless** (verified before building: 13 contract codes live, current to 2026-07-21). **Coverage is the headline and the verdict is subordinate to it.** Exactly **2 of 10** positions in the live book map to a futures contract — SHY (UST 2Y) and SVXY (VIX) — which is **22.2% of gross**. A panel opening with "nothing is crowded" would have been true and deeply misleading, so `coverage_share` is computed, persisted and rendered first; `crowded_share <= coverage_share` holds by construction and is tested across crowded and uncrowded readings alike; both denominate in **book gross**, never the observed slice, or a single crowded position in a thinly covered book would read as "100% crowded". All 8 unmapped positions are stored in `unobservable` **with a reason**, keyed on `SECTOR_MAP` so a name added tomorrow inherits one the day it appears. **Miners are not the metal:** GDX is not mapped to gold futures and XLE not to crude — that would have raised coverage from 22% to roughly 35% and made the panel look far more useful, which is exactly why it is refused. **The inverse product is where this could have been silently wrong:** SVXY is a -0.5x VIX product, so long SVXY is a **short** volatility position; comparing its stated direction to the VIX reading without the flip gives a verdict that is exactly backwards, and a wrong *side* — unlike a wrong *number* — has no symptom a reader could catch, so a test asserts the flipped and unflipped comparisons **disagree** rather than merely checking the flip exists. That is the **third** finding this week whose substance was a direction nobody had stated (short GDX+NOC in ADR-0088, net-short sanctions in ADR-0096); the recurring failure is not arithmetic, it is an exposure that falls out of sizing and is never named. Agreeing with a crowd is **disclosure, never a signal** — exposure to that consensus unwinding — and nothing feeds sizing; the 80/20 threshold is the convention, named as a constant and explicitly not fitted. **The lag is carried:** CFTC positions are as of Tuesday and publish Friday, so `as_of` is the *observation* date (stalest across contracts), not retrieval. `fetched=false`, empty readings and a null column are **three distinct renders**, none of them "not crowded". Live readings were mid-range (UST 2Y 69.9, VIX 33.2). Backfilled onto the 2026-07-25 row and **logged in `book_revisions`** — the third real entry. **ETF flows were asked for and are NOT here:** `yfinance.get_shares_full()` returns **zero rows** for GLD/HYG/TLT/GDX (tested, not assumed), the alternatives are a paid feed or per-issuer scraping, and the free proxies are worse than nothing — volume is turnover, not flow, and calling it flow would be the exact lie the rest of this is built to prevent. Also closed a silent-failure class: adding an analytic takes four edits and missing the `/risk` select list produces **no error anywhere** (the panel renders its empty state forever while the value sits in the database), so `risk-analytics-columns.test.ts` derives the expectation from `ResearchAnalyticsRow` itself and was negative-tested. Both fell out of picks made on entirely other grounds and neither was mentioned. **The valuable half needs no credential** — which names sit in a sanctions-sensitive jurisdiction and which side we hold them is entirely ours (`GEO_MAP` + picks). | 2026-07-26 | **S6 can now be driven by measured disruption instead of a constant — and finding out whether the data was reachable was most of the work.** [ADR-0095](docs/adrs/0095-s6-scales-by-measured-disruption-when-there-is-a-reading.md). ADR-0088's sector map is fixed, so the stress table read the same whether Hormuz was quiet or closed. **Probed before building:** worldmonitor's REST API is gated (`api.worldmonitor.app/v1/...` returns the SPA shell; other paths 403), **but their MCP endpoint answers `initialize` and `tools/list` unauthenticated** — 41 tools, including exactly the two I had flagged as valuable, `get_chokepoint_status` and `get_sanctions_data`. `tools/call` then returns **`-32001 Authentication required`**. So **discovery is free and execution is not**, and the cheapest credential is the **Pro tier at $39.99/mo** (it lists MCP connectors) — correcting the $99.99 API-Starter figure I gave earlier in this session. Crucially the tool declares an **`outputSchema`**, so the adapter is written against a documented contract rather than a guessed shape — that is the line between building an adapter and inventing data the backend does not have. **Design decisions worth keeping:** *scale, don't replace* — `BASELINE_DISRUPTION_PCT = 40` is the disruption ADR-0088 already represents, so at 40% the multiplier is exactly 1.0 and the reviewed calibration stays the reference point rather than being silently rescaled; **bounded 0.5x–1.5x**, because an upstream reporting 100% must not produce a 2.5x shock nobody signed off, and the clamp is disclosed when it binds; **magnitude only, never direction**, since a worse supply shock hurts importers more and helps producers more but reverses nobody; **the worst chokepoint binds, not the average**, or a quiet Panama would cancel a closed Hormuz. Every refusal — `stale`, `dataAvailable: false`, non-numeric, out of range, no key, network error, upstream error — returns the neutral 1.0 **with a stated reason**, never a bare 1.0 a reader would mistake for a measurement. Their feed distinguishes **transport freshness (`stale`) from content availability (`dataAvailable`)** — the same split the `assessStaleness` work and ADR-0094 arrived at independently — and both are honoured: a stale cache is not a low-disruption reading, and "cannot see" is not "all clear". **Caught myself writing a comment about a defect instead of fixing it:** I noted that `scenario_results_to_dict` keying off the module constant would persist a scaled P&L beside the *unscaled* description, then left the code that way. It now takes the run's scenarios (defaulting to the constant, so no caller breaks), with `run_scenario_analysis_with_scenarios` returning both halves — and a test that asserts the two disagree if you resolve against the constant. `SCENARIOS` is never mutated; pinned. **The live path is implemented but UNVERIFIED** — no credential, so `fetch_signal` has never returned real data. The worldmonitor review proposed adopting its corroboration requirement (fire only when N independent origin TYPES agree) as a gate on theme promotion — a genuinely different guard from HypeScore's weighted blend, where one loud source can carry a theme. Checked how many source types exist before writing any of it: `select source, count(*) from theme_news` returns **`brave | 1005`** and nothing else. Reddit **is** fetched and tagged by `daily_refresh`, but `REDDIT_CLIENT_ID` is not among the repo secrets — it is not even in CLAUDE.md's own secrets table — so `reddit_client` returns `[]` rather than fabricating posts, which is [ADR-0023](docs/adrs/0023-data-provenance-and-fabrication-guard.md)'s fabrication guard working exactly as designed. **So HypeScore's attention, volume and sentiment all rest on one provider — and four surfaces claimed otherwise:** two "Brave News + Reddit" table cells, *"All collected text came from live Brave/Reddit responses"*, and `provenanceLabel`'s *"Live Brave/Reddit signal"*. A provider that supplies none of the rows, named on the pages whose entire job is to say where a number came from. **Refused the gate.** At one source type it is not implementable in any honest form: N=1 is a control that never refuses anything, N>=2 is a control that empties the candidate pool. Either way it asserts a property the corpus does not have — [ADR-0059](docs/adrs/0059-a-single-date-ic-is-not-validation.md)'s single-date-IC failure in another costume, and the class of thing this repo keeps deleting. **Disclosed the count instead, derived from the data**: `sourceIndependence()` computes the contributing providers, the independent count, the top provider's share, which pipeline-fetched providers contributed nothing, and which contributed only fallback rows; `independenceSentence()` renders one actionable sentence; `/method/evidence#sources` shows it with a per-provider table. Derived rather than hardcoded on purpose — the day Reddit credentials land, the disclosure corrects itself instead of becoming a new false claim pointing the other way. `PIPELINE_SOURCES` is the one constant, because it is the only way to tell *"returned nothing today"* from *"never asked"*. Details that matter: `topSourceShare` is **null, not 1.0**, on an empty corpus (a share of nothing is unmeasurable, ADR-0066); an absent provider renders **em dash, not 0**; a `mock_` source counts as **present but synthetic**, so "the stub ran" and "the provider was silent" stay distinguishable. The guardrail did its job and the right book is live; what was missing was any record that a choice had been made. `_record_book_revisions` now read-then-diffs **before** the upsert and **non-fatally** — it reports on the book, it does not produce one, so a logging failure must never cost a run ready to publish. **`reason` is NOT NULL** with a non-blank check, defaulting to a stated non-attribution rather than an empty string: an unexplained revision reads exactly like the silent overwrite the table exists to prevent, the same rule as `pick_outcomes.void_requires_reason` and `assessStaleness.unjudgeableReason`. Position identity is `(asset, direction)` per ADR-0040, so a long→short flip is a different position and never a weight edit — reporting it as one would hide a reversed bet. | 2026-07-26 | **Shipped the worldmonitor backlog's cheapest item and its most valuable one: the book now renders its own track record, and answers to anyone's agent.** Two commits. **(1) `/method/evidence#track-record`** makes [ADR-0090](docs/adrs/0090-a-published-pick-must-be-falsifiable.md)'s instrument public: hit rate, mean signed return, void rate, pending count with first expected maturity, split by direction. **Shipped while it has nothing to report** — live it reads `—` on every outcome-dependent figure, 23 awaiting resolution, first maturity 2026-08-20 — because a scorecard that appears once there are results is a scorecard whose test was chosen after the fact. `lib/method/trackRecord.ts` re-implements `build_scorecard` in TS rather than sharing it; normally a second copy is what this codebase refuses ([ADR-0064](docs/adrs/0064-the-audit-page-blamed-the-pipeline-for-its-own-arithmetic.md)), and the justification is that the backend aggregate is **never persisted** — only per-pick rows are — so the alternative is not "share the function" but "persist a summary and let it go stale against its own rows". Both sides pin the same four semantics; `trackRecordStatus` adds a **`too-thin`** state below 20 resolved picks, which is [ADR-0059](docs/adrs/0059-a-single-date-ic-is-not-validation.md)'s failure restated. **(2) `POST /api/mcp` + `/llms.txt`** ([ADR-0092](docs/adrs/0092-the-book-as-an-mcp-server-over-the-tools-that-already-exist.md)) — ADR-0087 built read-only tools importing the **same modules the pages render**, and they were reachable only through our own LLM. The MCP endpoint exposes that identical registry over **Streamable HTTP** at the minimum shape the spec permits: POST only with `GET`→405 (no server-initiated stream, so no `listChanged` — the registry is a module constant), JSON not SSE, stateless, `Origin`-validated (a MUST for the transport — otherwise a page the reader merely visits could drive it from their machine). **No rate limit, deliberately:** `/ask` is capped because it spends the MiniMax quota that writes the nightly book; here the caller brings their own model and the reads are anon-key reads of rows the static pages already serve. `/llms.txt` generates its tool list from `TOOLS` so it cannot drift — the case for it was empirical, since worldmonitor's own `llms.txt` let one fetch enumerate ~200 operations that crawling HTML would have taken many. **I loaded the MCP spec rather than working from memory**, which was right: the `claude-api` skill covers Claude *as* an MCP client, not the wire format for implementing a server, so the transport rules, the `initialize` negotiation and the `tools/call` result shape came from modelcontextprotocol.io. **Two defects found by running it, not reading it.** The text block emitted `Positions in the book: 10 count` and `Gross exposure: 0.5933244069596506 pct` — `f.unit` is a **type tag**, not a display suffix, so `displayFact` moved from module-private to exported and both surfaces now render a figure identically (the exact defect `lib/chat/tools.ts` opens by warning about). And the diagram's `ROUTE` node still claimed to be *"the ONLY route handler in the repo"*, which three handlers later is false — the CLAUDE.md rule about a future agent learning something wrong from the diagram, caught before commit rather than after. **Verified live through the full client handshake**: negotiated `2025-06-18`, notification→202, 9 tools, `book_summary`→**33 facts**, `position_detail{asset:"largest_short"}`→PDD with 23 facts and thesis prose, unknown tool→`-32602`. **444 frontend tests across 37 files (+40), tsc clean, lint clean.** Still open on the backlog, in order: the provenance envelope (`known`/`unknown`/`not_applicable` with required reasons), a corrections/revision log, a corroboration gate on theme promotion, and COT/ETF-flow crowding evidence. |

Recorded live (idempotent: ran twice, still 23 rows / 23 distinct claims). Wired non-fatally into `daily-refresh.yml` after the refresh so today's book is logged the day it publishes. **Reading the ARCHITECTURE checklist caught a bug in my own script**: it printed a Unicode `·` before the upsert, which is exactly the defect [ADR-0059](docs/adrs/0059-a-single-date-ic-is-not-validation.md) hit in `backtest_hype` (froze the `/method` IC panel) and the ADR-0065 follow-up hit in `check_data_integrity` — `UnicodeEncodeError` on a cp1252 stdout, killing the run *before* it writes. You cannot buy a latency edge with a daily batch job, and real-time is an explicit non-goal. Their **visual** language fails `design-goals.md` triage at step 2 outright — `#0a0a0a` ground with neon green spent on brand, primary CTA, active chip *and* hover, which is goal 3's named failure mode at full saturation, plus a viewport-locked panel grid (goal 7) and an emoji glyph rail. **What did transfer is one structural idea**: their `GetSectorDependency` / `GetMultiSectorCostShock` services model a disruption→sector-cost transmission, and we had no such channel. **The gap that found**: [ADR-0074](docs/adrs/0074-stress-both-tails-not-just-the-crash.md) gave the stress suite its second *tail* but it still had one *channel* — S1–S5 are each a market shock scaled by a factor beta, so **a position the factor model cannot see is invisible to the entire battery**. Measured on the live 2026-07-25 book: **NOC**, `beta_mkt` **−0.014**, a 4.73% short whose entire investment case is geopolitical — a short in defense is a short in escalation — had a worst case of **9 basis points across all five scenarios**; **SHY**, the book's largest long at 9.25%, scored **2.6bp** for the same reason. **23.6% of gross was effectively unstressed, structurally rather than by oversight.** The suite also had no inflationary shock, so a duration hedge looked sound against every downside on the page (TLT is +4%/+6% in S1/S4). **Built `S6_supply_shock`**, which transmits through the `SECTOR_MAP` buckets the book is already capped and reported against rather than through beta: `Scenario` gains `sector_shocks`, resolution is ticker → sector → factor through a single `_resolve_shock` that the P&L loop and the `covered_frac` gate now **share** — they could previously disagree about what "covered" meant, and a gate counting an asset the loop skips silently selects the wrong estimator. `factor_shocks` is deliberately near-empty (`mkt` −0.04); routing it through the beta channel too would have collapsed it into a smaller S1. **It scores +1.48% and does *not* become the worst case** — the melt-up stays worst at −1.99%, the book is short 18% of China and a chokepoint shock hurts China — and it was **not tuned to produce a scary number**, because a scenario built for a predetermined headline is not a stress test. **What it changes is coverage**: 10/10 positions take a direct shock, NOC moves 9bp → **47bp**, and the book's implicit short-geopolitical-risk position — short gold miners **and** short defense, **−1.24%** combined — becomes visible for the first time. SHY moves 2.6bp → 5bp, and the point is not the magnitude but that a *measured* near-zero and an *unmeasured* one render identically while meaning different things (goal 2, [ADR-0066](docs/adrs/0066-not-computable-must-persist-as-null.md)). **The five existing scenarios reproduce their persisted values to the basis point** (`+0.00pp` on all five, re-run against the live book with its real `factor_exposures`) — checked precisely because the first run diverged, which turned out to be my harness passing `factor_exposures=None`, not the change. Sector transmission also generalises where a ticker list does not: a name added to `SECTOR_MAP` is stressed the day it appears, pinned by a test that fails if any Tier-1 ticker resolves no shock. `/risk` now renders chips from **both** maps — a sector-transmitted scenario emitting only `factor_shocks` would have shown a near-empty chip row under a material P&L, a number with its cause left off — and breakdown rows name their origin (`NOC (short) via Defense`). Sub-percent shocks print at 1dp: at the table's `:+.0%`, SHY's −0.5% rendered `+0%`, a row whose own numbers multiply to zero under a non-zero result, which is the self-consistency the ADR-0074-era rows were explicitly fixed to keep. **Persisted to the live book, append-only.** Recomputing all six would have rewritten the five published returns by ~**2e-08** — float4 betas widened to float64 differ from the in-memory regression output the original run used — and a rounding artefact is not a reason to touch a published number, so only the S6 object was appended. The five are **byte-identical across the write** (md5 of elements 1–5 unchanged at `1dffa47…`), and the `UPDATE` was guarded on that md5 *and* on S6 not already being present, so it cannot double-apply or race the other session. Order is irrelevant because `/risk` re-sorts with `sortWorstFirst`. Confirmed live on production: six rows, worst-first, S6 at **+1.48% / +$1.5M / LOW** with all ten legs and their `via Energy` origins rendering (`docs/captures/2026-07-26/risk-s6-{live,breakdown-live}.png`). **One gap until the frontend deploys**: the shipped `ShockChips` reads `factor_shocks` only, so S6 currently shows a lone `MKT -4.00%` chip instead of its 22 sector chips — incomplete rather than false (`mkt` −0.04 *is* one of its shocks, and the description says the transmission is sectoral), but it is the goal-1 shape this change set out to remove, and it closes when the component ships. **Verified: 46 scenario tests (+9 new), 633 backend passed, 392 frontend across 35 files, tsc clean.** The 16 `test_theme_discovery` failures are pre-existing and environmental (`ModuleNotFoundError: gensim`) — confirmed by stashing every file I touched and watching them fail identically. **Also found, not fixed:** `docs/adrs/README.md` carries **40 double-encoded em-dashes and one `§` committed in HEAD** (`â€"`), pre-existing and untouched here because the other session is live in that file and a 40-instance encoding repair under a concurrent editor is its own change. **Deferred with reasons in the ADR:** their forecast scorecard (machine-checkable resolution specs, Brier/log score, calibration buckets, **VOID** rate) is the biggest gap we have — we backtest signals but publish no forward record of the books we shipped, which is the same hole the questflow review logged as `pick_outcomes`; their decision-signal provenance envelope (`known`/`unknown`/`not_applicable` with a *required reason*, four role-distinct timestamps, and transport-freshness kept separate from content-freshness) is a typed vocabulary for what goals 1 and 2 currently enforce by convention; and an `llms.txt` + MCP over the read-only tools already in `lib/chat/tools.ts` clears the same bar `/ask` cleared. Their positioning/flow data (`GetCotPositioning`, `ListEtfFlows` — *external* crowding evidence, against our internal HHI) is more relevant to a long-short book than any conflict feed. **Note for whoever picks that up: ACLED is not free for commercial use**; GDELT is the open substitute and they wrap it too. |

| 2026-07-25 | **The scheduled daily job stopped finishing, and nothing noticed.** Re-deriving found `/risk` computing on **38 provisional positions** with no book published for 2026-07-24. `record_pipeline_run` maps its internal `"started"` sentinel to the DB status **`partial`**, updating it to success/failure on completion - so `partial` means *began and has not finished*: the transient state **during** a run and the **permanent** state of a run that died, with nothing distinguishing the two. **2026-07-23 L5 succeeded in 2.5 minutes (22:31:45 -> 22:34:13); 2026-07-24 L5 started 22:34:58 and never wrote a terminal status.** No book was published for that date, the site served the previous run's, and pipeline_runs held `partial` indefinitely. **That run was the scheduled one, not a local one** - it stamped `run_date` 2026-07-24, the UTC date, which this UTC+8 machine could not produce before [ADR-0069](docs/adrs/0069-run-date-is-utc-not-the-local-clock.md), since at 22:34 UTC its `date.today()` was already 07-25. **For a deliverable whose Q2 claim is "a daily process", a daily job that silently stops finishing is the failure that matters most** - and every guard built so far checks whether the *data* is consistent, none asked whether the *run happened*. `check_stalled_stages` flags any stage still `partial` beyond the window, and **the threshold has a stated basis rather than a fitted one**: `daily-refresh.yml` sets `timeout-minutes: 60`, so no legitimately-running stage can be older than that (GitHub kills the job first), and 90 minutes is that ceiling plus a 30-minute margin for a delayed scheduler and clock skew - **a run genuinely in progress can never trip it and a dead one always will**. Silent on no rows, no `started_at`, an unparseable timestamp, or a terminal status; a stage inside the window is *running*, not stalled, which would otherwise fire on every concurrent run **including the one this was written during**. A naive timestamp is read as UTC, since treating it as local would shift the age by the writer's offset - the same class of bug ADR-0069 just fixed. Also re-ran the pipeline to complete 2026-07-24, which the scheduled run left unpublished. 554 backend tests. **Twelfth deploy refusal**, so the headroom fix remains committed and not live. |

Contradicting an accepted ADR without noticing is reason enough to check which one the code has been doing. Every `L0` run on record, against the UTC date of its own start: **2026-07-23T22:30 -> 2026-07-23 (== UTC)**, **2026-07-24T21:43 -> 2026-07-25 (forward +1)**, **2026-07-24T22:34 -> 2026-07-24 (== UTC)**. **The only forward-dated run in the entire history is the middle one - my own local run from a UTC+8 machine.** The scheduled job has always stamped its own UTC date; there was never any code that added a day. **So the convention was inferred from a bug**: ADR-0062 was written while the artifact rows my local runs had just created were the freshest data in the table, and `run_date` 07-25 on data written 07-24T20:13Z is exactly what forward-dating would look like *and* exactly what `date.today()` on a UTC+8 machine looks like. **ADR-0062's fix is untouched and still correct** - sourcing the landing header from `pipeline_runs.run_date` rather than `themes.updated_at` was right because a write timestamp is not a run identifier - so only the *description* is corrected, by annotating its status line rather than rewriting its body (the ADR-0052 precedent). **Nothing renamed, no rows rewritten:** the artifact row stays until the 07-25 scheduled run overwrites it by upsert, with /risk's ADR-0040 reconciliation covering the page meanwhile. **The sharp lesson: a convention read off live data is only as trustworthy as the data** - two agents produced an artifact and the artifact was written down as intent; three rows and one query reversed the story. **Derive a convention from the code and its history, not from the newest row.** [ADR-0070](docs/adrs/0070-forward-dating-was-never-implemented.md). |

Scoped to the run *identifier*; other `date.today()` uses are durations where a one-day shift is not a correctness problem. **The regression test asserts the source, not the value** - comparing against `date.today()` passes on any UTC runner, which is where CI runs, so it could never catch a reversion. 549 backend tests. **A correction I nearly published:** before finding the cause I read `/risk`, saw no provisional warning, and was one step from reporting the ADR-0040 check as broken - it was firing correctly and **my regex required a `.` within 200 characters**. Verify the entry point that runs, not a convenient one. **And it would not have been loud:** the workflow wraps the step in `|| echo "::warning::"`, so a crash and a real integrity flag are indistinguishable - both a warning, the step never red - and nothing separated *the guard says the data is bad* from *the guard never ran*. Fixed with the `sys.path.insert` every other script here already carries. **A second defect, found by writing the test rather than by reading the code:** the guard prints check marks, and on a cp1252 console (any Windows shell without `PYTHONIOENCODING`) that raises `UnicodeEncodeError` and exits 1, which the same fallback turns into a warning that looks like a real flag - **a guard that dies formatting its own verdict is worse than one that says nothing**. `stdout`/`stderr` are reconfigured to UTF-8 defensively. Two tests pin the **entry point** rather than the function, asserting no `ModuleNotFoundError`, no `UnicodeEncodeError` and no traceback from a subprocess run of the script; the exit code accepts **0 or 2 deliberately**, because `load_dotenv()` searches from the script's directory upward so credentials cannot be stripped from a subprocess and the code differs locally (0) from CI (2) - pinning either would assert where the test runs rather than what it tests. **Verified under both invocations against production**, exit 0, all three checks green. 530 backend tests. **The general lesson, twice today:** a test can pass while the thing it protects is broken, if it exercises a different path than production - [ADR-0063](docs/adrs/0063-one-beta-bar-across-every-surface.md) was a panel that never saw a sample size; this is a guard that never saw its own entry point. **Left unchanged deliberately:** the `|| echo` wrapper, since making the guard a hard failure would red the daily run for a second agent working in this repo - but it is why this would have gone unnoticed, and **a guard whose step is always green is a guard nobody reads**. |

It **reuses** `q1_agent`'s functions rather than reimplementing them, because a second copy of a guardrail is a second thing to drift - precisely [ADR-0064](docs/adrs/0064-the-audit-page-blamed-the-pipeline-for-its-own-arithmetic.md). **Also re-derived:** the pool-depth measurement has **N=1** - `independent_ideas` was added on 2026-07-25 and `trade_candidates` holds only the latest run, so it cannot be backfilled; it will accumulate per `run_date` going forward, but every claim that the short side *has 5 independent ideas* currently rests on one observation. **Verified against production:** *Published thesis for 2026-07-25 makes no contradicted claim (checked against 40 screened candidates)*, exit 0; negative control is a unit test carrying the exact false sentence that was live. 528 backend tests. **Deploy still refused** (fourth attempt), and the Playwright browser became unstable mid-iteration so `/risk` could not be re-checked - recorded rather than glossed. | 2026-07-25 | **The audit page was accusing the pipeline of a data-integrity failure; the pipeline was right and the page arithmetic was wrong.** `/method` - the Q2 surface, whose purpose is to show every published number is reproducible from the published formula - carried a red **RECONCILIATION FAILURE**: *Applying the live weights to the persisted components yields +0.1700, but theme_signals_history.edge_score holds +0.3542. Either the weights changed after this row was written, or a component column and the score column were not written from the same inputs.* **Both stated causes are false.** `0.169999 / (0.20 + 0.23 + 0.05) = 0.354165` - the persisted value, exactly. [ADR-0036](docs/adrs/0036-carry-as-excess-yield-over-funding.md) established that a `null` component is *not computable* and is **dropped, with its weight renormalised over the components present**, which is what `compute_edge_score` does. The page kept summing `w x (v ?? 0)`, kept printing `(null -> 0)`, and when the answer disagreed it **published an accusation against the pipeline**. The backend was changed; the page never was. **Same root cause on `/book`:** `EdgeBars` sums `edgeContributions`, which applies the identical `?? 0`, and warns when the total differs from the persisted score by >0.01 - so every position whose theme lacks a carry or value proxy raised the same false alarm inside its own derivation. One bug, two surfaces. **This is the sharpest form of the failure this project keeps finding: not a wrong number in isolation, but an AUDITING surface whose own arithmetic was wrong, presenting its error as the audited system error** - a reviewer checking the maths would have concluded the pipeline is unreliable, on the page own reasoning. `recomputeEdgeScore` implements ADR-0036 in one place and returns **the parts, not just the total**, because a worked example that prints only the answer proves nothing; the block now shows the weighted sum, the weight present, and the quotient, and a dropped component renders *(dropped, weight redistributed)*. The explanatory note is corrected too: it claimed a null component *contributes exactly 0 - an honest absence, not a zero tilt*, which is both a contradiction in terms and the opposite of ADR-0036. **`edgeContributions` is deliberately unchanged** - `?? 0` is correct for *ranking* which component dominates, where a null must never win; only the surfaces that reconcile against a persisted score were wrong. The failure panel **keeps its trigger and wording** - it was doing its job, fed a wrong number, and removing it would have been the wrong fix. **Worth stating plainly:** every previous finding here corrected a page that was too flattering; this one corrects a page that was **unfairly damning**. 116 frontend tests. [ADR-0064](docs/adrs/0064-the-audit-page-blamed-the-pipeline-for-its-own-arithmetic.md). |

| 2026-07-25 | **`/risk` said one position was −1071% of the book.** The per-position attribution's **Net share** column read −1071.4% (PDD), −1019.0% (BABA), +975.7% (XLE), +711.9% (NUE), +686.6% (JPM). Nothing to fit and nothing to drift, unlike [ADR-0047](docs/adrs/0047-conviction-needs-a-vol-floor.md)'s vol floor which needed a stated basis. **Withheld rather than clamped:** capping the display at 100% would still assert the decomposition exists, when the honest statement is that a near-market-neutral book's directional tilt does not decompose — the same distinction iterations 20–21 drew for VaR/Sharpe below their minimum sample (*"we do not know"* and *"we know, and it is X"* must not look alike). The predicate is **exported and used by both the computation and the note that explains the blank**, so the column and its explanation cannot disagree — the drift [ADR-0058](docs/adrs/0058-explanations-are-owed-per-empty-slot.md) hit when a verdict was re-derived at the render layer. The note names both figures and states this is the **normal** state of a long-short book rather than a data outage, because a bare `—` with no explanation is the defect `GOAL.md` tells every iteration to hunt. **Gross share is untouched and is the column to read.** On a genuinely directional book the column returns with no code change. **Found by reading the rendered page** — no test would have caught it: the old code computed exactly what it intended, and there was no disagreement between two numbers, only a number that could not mean what its label claimed. **Named rather than fixed:** `Avg |Ï| to book` renders `—` on all nine rows because it averages only pairs flagged at Ï ≥ 0.70 and this book has none. 101 frontend tests. Only a shortfall is flagged — caps can only reduce, and an overshoot is a louder bug the cap step already surfaces. Five tests, including the exact live 19.0/20/6.4 case. **This is the general guard the specific fix deserved**: ADR-0053 stops the book being sized by the wrong model; this catches any future divergence between the model shown and the weight held. |

| 2026-07-25 | **`LLM_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` never bounded the call — it bounded the gap between bytes.** The value was passed straight to `requests.post(timeout=...)`, which applies it to the connect and to the **inter-byte gap**; requests' own docs say it *"is not a time limit on the entire response download"*. A provider that trickles data — or a reasoning model thinking slowly with the connection alive — never trips it. **Observed, not theorised.** At 900s, one L5 call ran **23 minutes** and completed normally; a later one passed **45 minutes** still inside a single attempt and had to be killed by hand. That second one is why [ADR-0050](docs/adrs/0050-separate-agent-churn-from-market-churn.md)'s replication measurement produced no number in the iteration that built it — the first of four samples never returned. **The consequence that matters is not a slow script.** `daily-refresh.yml` runs this every weekday: a stalled provider call there is a **hung workflow that produces nothing** and holds the runner toward its 6-hour ceiling, with the stale-book banner the only downstream sign. Every retry and fallback path in [ADR-0012](docs/adrs/0012-citation-guardrail-llm-defense.md) sits downstream of a call that *returns*; none of it engages while a call simply never ends. A wall-clock deadline now wraps whichever provider runs, and the per-request timeout is kept as the inner guard it actually is. The worker is **abandoned rather than awaited** (it holds a socket; this is a batch process), and `TimeoutError` feeds the existing retry-then-fallback path — what should have happened all along. A fast provider error still surfaces as itself, pinned by a test, because the retry feedback depends on telling a 429 from a stall. **Three earlier raises of this value (120 → 420 → 900) were treating the wrong dial.** Not wasted — generation genuinely was slower than 120s — but "raise the timeout" was never going to fix a stall, and the fact that raising it *appeared* to help each time is exactly why the misreading survived. 452 tests. [ADR-0051](docs/adrs/0051-llm-timeout-bounded-silence-not-the-call.md). |
| 2026-07-25 | **Built the frozen-input replication harness — agent churn is not market churn.** `docs/GOAL.md` has carried this as an open measurement for many iterations *with the method already written down* (*"run the pipeline twice on frozen inputs and diff the positions"*, and *"do that before claiming the book is stable"*). Nothing had done it. The turnover panel ([ADR-0045](docs/adrs/0045-turnover-on-names-without-a-verdict.md)) measures day-over-day change, which conflates **the market moved** with **the agent changed its mind**. Only the second is a credibility problem, and no amount of staring at day-over-day turnover separates them. `scripts/replication_test.py` builds the L5 state **once** through the deterministic nodes, then calls `reason_picks` N times on independent deep copies — same candidates, macro, regime, book metrics, scenarios, prompt, temperature — so whatever differs is the model. The pool is **read back from `trade_candidates`** rather than re-ranked, because re-running L1 would refetch prices and reintroduce the exact confound being removed. Reported **per side, each beside its independent-idea count** (ten ideas for five slots is a different situation from four filling four, and averaging them hides the explanation), plus which names survived every sample. Persisted to `backtest_results(test_name='book_replication')` and rendered on `/book` beneath the turnover panel, **no verdict attached** — following ADR-0045's rule. **What prompted it, and why it was not enough:** four runs stamped `run_date` 2026-07-25 within one hour showed consecutive turnover of **50% / 77% / 50%** — but code and prompt changed between several of them, so *none of it is admissible*. One detail did shape the design: between the last two, the **short side was identical** (SLV, BABA, PDD, NOC) while the **long side kept one name of five**, with pool depth at 4 short ideas and 10 long. Where the pool exactly fills the book the agent has no choice; where it over-fills, it chooses. [ADR-0050](docs/adrs/0050-separate-agent-churn-from-market-churn.md). |

The rule this is an instance of: **a number the system computes should never be re-typed by the model.** 449 tests. |

| 2026-07-25 | **Conviction was measuring the denominator, not the idea — and it is a sizing weight.** `conviction = |EdgeScore| / vol` (ADR-0032) both sizes the book and prints in the Conv. column on `/book`. Unfloored, **BIL — a 0-3 month T-bill ETF at 0.19% ANNUALISED realised vol — scored 2375.2x, 109x the next name and 260x SLV at 74.6%**. Thirty-five of thirty-nine names sat in a 9-30x band; the four that broke out were exactly the four below ~5% annualised vol. That is not a claim BIL was 260x the better idea. **Not only a display artefact**: `allocate_portfolio(size_by="conviction")` weights by this number, so a cash-like instrument absorbs the book until the single-name cap stops it — inverse-vol sizing pushed past the point where it scales risk and into simply seeking the least volatile thing available. Now `|EdgeScore| / max(vol, conviction_vol_floor)`, floor **0.00315 daily ≈ 5% annualised**. The basis is economic and stated rather than fitted: 5% annualised is the conventional line between a cash-like instrument and a risk position, and today's universe corroborates it (BIL 0.19% and SHY 1.50% are places to park money; IEF 5.07% and AGG 4.16% sit on the line). **The floor is ABSOLUTE, never a percentile of the day's names.** A relative floor would make conviction a statement about whatever happened to be scored alongside it — the defect [ADR-0042](docs/adrs/0042-absolute-hype-subscores.md) removed from HypeScore, where a theme moved 60.6 → 36.6 on byte-identical inputs — and a *sizing weight* with that property would rebalance the book on days nothing about the book changed. A zero vol now returns `|edge| / floor` instead of `|edge|`, which used to rank an unpriceable name at the **bottom** (|edge| ≤ 1 against a book of 9-30), reading as low conviction when the truth is no measurement. **Verified on a live pipeline run: BIL 2375.2 → 92.8, SHY 292.1 → 87.7, AGG 99.2 → 82.8, IEF unchanged at 79.6**, every above-floor name untouched — it is a floor, not a rescaling. Book-wide max/median **148x → under 6x**. Each sub-score now maps the theme's OWN signal against a documented anchor: volume `tanh(m7/3.0)`, sentiment unchanged, correlation `min(1, |corr|/0.50)`, momentum `(tanh(z/2.0)+1)/2`. The same two cases now move **−7.5 and −2.2** instead of −24.0 and −11.5 — the residual being each theme's genuine correlation decline — and their volume sub-scores are now identical across the two days, as an identical mention count should give. One input caused it: the regime moved `late/risk-on` → `late/neutral`, and `regime_direction_bias` reads that label through `_SENTIMENT_SIGN {+1, 0, −1}` times the asset class's risk beta, so an equity's regime term swung **+0.5β → −0.5β — a full 1.0β move on a component carrying 0.23 of EdgeScore**, inverting the equity complex at once. And the label moved for a trivial reason: with VIX 18.6 (failing `<15` and `<18`) and HY OAS 268bp (failing `<250`), the *only* rule that could return risk-on was `breadth > 60`. Breadth 61 and 59 are not different market states. The label is a good **summary** and a terrible **dial**, so it is now display-only: `risk_appetite(vix, hy_oas, vix_term_diff, breadth)` returns a continuous [−1, +1] score from smooth tanh terms centred on each input's neutral level, and direction uses that. Across the breadth threshold the score now moves **0.022 instead of 1.0**, while genuine regimes still separate — stress (VIX 32, HY 620, backwardation, breadth 25) scores −0.94 and calm (VIX 12, HY 240, contango, breadth 72) scores +0.80.
