(3) **Frontend**: new `DiscoveredThemes` on `/` ("What the engine is discovering") showing Tier-2 agreements + collapsed Tier-3, term chips, LDA/EMBEDDINGS badges, clearly labelled shadow (operator-promoted, never auto). +2 tests (14 discovery / 379 backend green). Verified live. |
| 2026-07-24 | **L5 picks carry `theme_id` + reconciled the q1_*/research_* doc naming drift.** (1) **theme_id on picks**: the L5 agent emitted picks by theme NAME only (`theme_id: null`) and the deterministic fallback dropped the id it had, so no stable theme key travelled with a pick — the `/book` focus deep-link had to match on the display name. New `_backfill_pick_theme_ids()` resolves each pick's theme name against the scored roster (case-insensitive) in the LLM path; the fallback now carries `c.theme_id` directly. Unmatched names (the LLM's 'other') stay id-less. +3 tests (377 backend green). (2) **Naming drift**: migration 008 renamed `q1_agent_runs`/`q1_recommendations` → `research_agent_runs`/`research_recommendations`, but the living design spec still described current storage with the old names, and the module-level LLM note said "Claude Sonnet" (the code is MiniMax→Anthropic→Gemini). Updated the spec (§14.8 storage + the L5 status line + open-questions) and the `update_spec_section.py` generator to the live names + real provider priority. ARCHITECTURE.md and CLAUDE.md were already correct. ADRs 0011/0012/0013/0015 (immutable) keep the old names in their bodies; added an authoritative "Naming note" mapping table to `docs/adrs/README.md` so a reader/grepper resolves old→live names without editing the decision records. |
| 2026-07-24 | **Abstained-theme "positions →" links no longer dead-end.** On the heatmap/cards, clicking "positions →" for a held-out theme landed on the full book (the `?theme=` param was ignored) showing the Energy positions with no hint the clicked theme has none. Now: the link label is honest (`held out →` for abstained themes vs `positions →`, via a shared `isThemeAbstained()` helper); the book honours `?theme=<id>` with a focus banner — a theme with positions gets "**X** holds N positions: TICKERS", a held-out theme gets "**X** is held out — no net-edge position, see the abstention roster" with a scroll link, and its roster row is highlighted + scrolled into view. Picks are matched by theme **name** (`p.theme`) since L5 output leaves `theme_id` null. Verified live: Inflation → held-out banner + highlighted roster row; Energy Prices → "holds 2 positions: XLE, CL". |
| 2026-07-24 | **HypeScore Volume = 7-day average mentions ([ADR-0035](docs/adrs/0035-volume-subscore-7day-average.md)) + risk-board HHI scale + Brave hang fix.** Three fixes. (1) **Volume degeneracy**: `mention_count_1d` was 0 for every theme on runs that collected no same-day article, so the Volume sub-score's cross-sectional min-max collapsed to a flat 0.5 → the heatmap showed a whole column of identical 50s. New shared `volume_base()` (7-day average, falls back to 1-day) feeds both `compute_hype_scores` and `persist`; Volume now = sustained attention level, Momentum = deviation from it. Re-scored prod from the latest stored signals (full `daily_refresh` blocked locally, see below): heatmap VOL now spans **0→100** (US Election 100, US Dollar 97, … Inflation 0). +4 tests (374 green). (2) **Risk-board HHI scale**: backend stores `concentration_hhi` on the 0–10 000 DOJ scale (2 500 for the 4-name book) but the limit default was 0.2 (0–1 scale) → the board read "1 250 000 % utilisation / BREACHED". Limit set to 2 000 (≈5 equal names), note + formatting corrected → now "2,500 vs 2,000 · 125% · −500 · BREACHED". (3) **Brave client hang**: `subprocess.run(text=True)` decoded the node Brave-MCP output with cp1252 on Windows; a non-ASCII headline byte (0x9d) crashed the stdout reader thread and hung the daily pipeline indefinitely. Now decodes UTF-8 with `errors="replace"`. NOTE: full local `daily_refresh` still stalls later in the L1 fetch/price stage on this Windows box (a separate subprocess/network-timeout hardening item); the volume fix was validated by re-scoring from stored `theme_signals_history`, which the next successful pipeline run reproduces identically. |
| 2026-07-24 | **Launch-polish pass — `/method` formulas fixed, Mentions populated, mobile layout ([ADR-0034](docs/adrs/0034-scoring-config-anon-read-policy.md)).** Driving the live site with Playwright surfaced three real defects. (1) **`/method` formula panels showed "NO ROWS"** for HypeScore/TradeScore/EdgeScore: `001` gave the seven display tables a `"Public read"` anon RLS policy but skipped `scoring_config`, so the frontend anon key read it as an empty set (backend unaffected — `service_role` bypasses RLS). Migration 027 adds the anon read policy (applied to prod); all 19 live weights now render. (2) **"Mentions —" was blank on every theme card**: `summarise()` fetched `mention_count_1d` but never surfaced it. Fixed to headline `mention_count_7d_avg` ("Mentions/day") since the 1-day count is structurally often 0 (a run frequently collects no same-day-dated article) — the stable windowed figure is the honest attention volume. (3) **Body horizontal scroll on phones**: the app-shell grid's implicit column defaulted to `auto`, sizing to the 640px position table's min-content and blowing past a 375px viewport; fixed with `grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)]` + a `min-w-0` wrapper on `{children}`, plus a tighter mobile TopBar. Desktop/tablet/phone verified clean via Playwright. |
| 2026-07-24 | **EdgeScore Stage 5 — sentiment demotion + IC-fit weights ([ADR-0033](docs/adrs/0033-edge-stage5-sentiment-ic-weights.md)).** Completes the 5-stage redesign. (1) Sentiment re-enters as a MINOR CONTRARIAN 5th component: `sentiment_signal = -tanh(avg_sentiment/0.4)` — extreme optimism is crowding to fade, not the coin-flip direction driver it wrongly was; small weight. (2) A multi-component IC backtest (`scripts/backtest_edge.py`) computes each component's rank IC vs forward 1m returns and **the data overturned a prior**: **Carry IC +0.277 (t=2.77, p=0.007, 60.6% hit) — strong & significant**, while **Trend is weak (+0.033, p=0.30)**. Weights shrunk 50% toward priors (thin data → no overfit) → migration 026 rebalances `edge_carry_weight` 0.20→0.34, `edge_trend_weight` 0.35→0.20, adds `edge_sentiment_weight` 0.05; RegimeFit/Sentiment keep priors (not IC-testable yet). Persisted `sentiment_signal` on `theme_signals_history`/positions/candidates (migration 026); frontend `edgeContributions` + `/method` worked example + drawer now reconcile all 5 components. Full backend suite + `next build` pass. |
| 2026-07-24 | **Never surface mock news (data integrity).** A `mock_brave` headline ("Investors eye US Election…") showed on the frontend despite `ANDROMEDA_ALLOW_MOCK=0`: `persist_theme_news` upserted on `(theme_id,run_date,headline)` and never cleared the run's prior rows, so mock from an earlier same-day (pre-flag) run lingered beside the real Brave data and was faithfully surfaced by the new raw-headline drill-down. Fix: idempotent re-run (prune the run_date before writing) + defense-in-depth (never PERSIST a `mock_*` source when mock is off). Live data cleaned (24 stale mock rows removed; `theme_news` = 498 real Brave, 0 mock; `data_source` = real/none). |
| 2026-07-23 | **Frontend caught up to the 4-component EdgeScore (closes the follow-up flagged below) + legacy pages retired.** `/method` §4 now renders the full `w_trend·Trend + w_regime·Regime + w_carry·Carry + w_value·Value` formula with **all four weights read live** from `scoring_config` (`edge_{trend,regime,carry,value}_weight`), the abstention rule (`\|EdgeScore\| < edge_abstain_threshold`), the conviction × inverse-vol sizing note, a worked example that reconciles all four persisted components to `edge_score`, and a new **abstention roster** table (themes with `\|EdgeScore\| < threshold` this run, sorted by gap-to-trade). `TradeDerivationDrawer` replaced its stale 2-component/0.6·0.4 block with the 4-bar decomposition (each component × live weight, null → 0 flagged), a `conviction = \|EdgeScore\|/vol` panel, the abstention line, and conviction-normalised sizing (Σ\|conviction\| denominator from `portfolio_positions.conviction`) in place of `HypeScore/100`; defaults now fall back to `DEFAULT_EDGE_WEIGHTS` from `frontend/lib/themeSignals.ts`, never hardcoded. **Legacy `/trades`, `/portfolio`, `/research`** — which duplicated the triad and (for `/trades`) bypassed the L5 citation guardrail — are now thin server `redirect()` → `/book` (idiomatic Next.js), so bookmarks and inbound links resolve to the consolidated, guardrail-verified surface. Frontend-only; owned files typecheck + lint clean. |
| 2026-07-23 | **EdgeScore Stages 3–4 — carry, value, abstention, conviction sizing ([ADR-0032](docs/adrs/0032-edge-carry-value-abstention-sizing.md)).** EdgeScore is now 4 components: `0.35·Trend + 0.25·RegimeFit + 0.20·Carry + 0.20·Value`. **Carry** (`carry_signal`) = L0 yield/spread income (credit HY OAS, rates real yield, fx USD rate); **Value** (`value_signal`) = z-score of L0 levels vs `macro_daily_history` (wide/cheap = long). **Abstention**: `rank_trade_candidates(abstain_threshold=0.15)` drops themes whose \|EdgeScore\| < 0.15 — a weak/conflicting signal yields no position, not a forced one. **Conviction sizing**: `allocate_portfolio(size_by="conviction")` weights ∝ `\|EdgeScore\|/vol` (conviction × inverse-vol), not HypeScore. Migration 024 rebalances the weights + adds `carry_signal`/`value_signal`/`conviction` columns. Real-data check (regime late/risk-on): 4 themes carry conviction, **4 abstain** — Corporate Credit abstains on a carry(+0.59)-vs-value(−0.75) conflict; US Dollar abstains on a flat signal. +10 tests (367 total). Stage 5 (sentiment demotion + IC-weighted composite) remains. NOTE: `/method` §4 + `TradeDerivationDrawer` now show only trend+regime and the 0.6/0.4 weights — a frontend follow-up should add carry/value and read the 4 rebalanced weights. |
| 2026-07-23 | **EdgeScore direction surfaced (was actively mis-explained).** After ADR-0031 made direction `sign(EdgeScore)` (trend + regime, not sentiment), the frontend still taught the superseded rule: `/method` §3 stated "the sign of TradeScore picks the side" and `TradeDerivationDrawer` showed "TradeScore > 0 → LONG" — a correctness/trust bug, since the backend row's own `entry_thesis` already reads "direction from EdgeScore". Corrected both to reframe TradeScore as intra-side ranking. Added a live `/method` §4 (EdgeScore formula with `scoring_config` weights + a reconciling worked example), a per-position "why this side" EdgeScore breakdown on `/book`, and the EdgeScore basis in the trade drawer. New `fetchThemeEdge` in `frontend/lib/themeSignals.ts` reads `edge_score/trend_signal/regime_bias` (migration 023). Verified against live data: 8 themes carry real EdgeScores that reconcile to `0.6·trend + 0.4·regime`. |
| 2026-07-23 | **EdgeScore — direction from trend + regime, not sentiment ([ADR-0031](docs/adrs/0031-edge-score-direction-signal.md), [spec](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-23-edge-score-direction-redesign.md)).** Direction was `sign(TradeScore) ≈ sign(near-zero VADER sentiment)` — a noise-level basis for a $100M book. New `backend/services/edge_signals.py` computes `EdgeScore = 0.6·Trend + 0.4·RegimeFit` (both [-1,1]): **Trend** = theme basket ~6-month price momentum (tanh-squashed); **RegimeFit** = `risk_beta(asset_class)·sentiment_sign + cycle_tilt` from the L3 regime (risk-off ⇒ long rates/USD, short equity/HY; late-cycle defensive). `rank_trade_candidates` now directions/ranks on `score_key="edge_score"`. Migration 023 adds `edge_score`/`trend_signal`/`regime_bias` columns + config weights. **IC-checked** (`scripts/backtest_edge.py`): Trend rank IC **+0.033** over 6y / 15-ETF panel — positive but weak (p=0.30, hit 54.9%), honestly caveated; wired in because it's economically anchored vs sentiment noise, weights to be IC-fit in Stage 5. Full 5-stage design specced; Stages 3–5 (carry, value, abstention, IC-weighting) planned. +17 tests (357 total). |
| 2026-07-23 | **Unified the L5 candidate pool with L1 ([ADR-0030](docs/adrs/0030-unify-l5-candidate-pool-with-l1.md)).** Found while tracing "how does the system decide long/short": the L5 agent had its *own* copy of the direction rule. `run_q1_agent` received the L1 pool but `screen_candidates` overwrote it, rebuilding from `theme_scores` with its own `hype>=threshold ∩ sign(TradeScore)`, no backfill — so ADR-0029's two-sided fix reached the `trade_candidates`/`portfolio_positions` tables but **not** the L5 `research_recommendations` book. A two-sided L5 book only happened by luck (US Election drifting 49.9→52.0 over the gate). `screen_candidates` now *filters* the L1 pool (inheriting the hype gate, direction, and two-sided backfill) and applies only the L5-specific lens + R² + dedupe + cap. Also unifies the asset universe onto the DB `theme_assets` mapping (was a hardcoded `_theme_default_assets`). The deterministic fallback now ranks the same screened pool instead of rebuilding from `theme_scores` (an adversarial review caught that the old rebuild could fabricate a one-sided book on LLM failure). +10 regression tests (341 total). |
| 2026-07-23 | **Two-sided book — decouple direction from the hype gate + revive momentum ([ADR-0029](docs/adrs/0029-two-sided-book-decouple-direction-revive-momentum.md)).** A run produced a 100% net-short, zero-long book. Cause: `rank_trade_candidates` built `long_pool = {hype≥50} ∩ {trade_score>0}`, and that day both hype-eligible themes (China Growth 90, US Dollar 71.8) were mildly bearish while every positive-signal theme sat below the gate → empty intersection. Compounding it, `hype_momentum` was 0 for every theme: `compute_trade_scores` sought a prior row dated *exactly* D-1, so irregular cadence + NULL historical `hype_score` left momentum dead and direction decided by fourth-decimal sentiment noise. Fix: (1) eligible-first ranking with `min_side` backfill from the strongest sub-threshold themes — two-sided whenever opposite-sign signal exists, never fabricated, backfills sized small by hype-weighting; (2) momentum reads the most-recent prior `hype_score` (self-heals once the daily job persists one). +6 backend tests (331 total). |
| 2026-07-23 | **L5 crash fix — `finalise_book_analytics` None-format.** The daily GitHub Action's L5 stage died with `unsupported format string passed to NoneType.__format__`: `worst = min(estimated_book_return for each scenario)` returned `None` when a sized pick had no factor beta *and* no direct scenario shock (unestimable scenario), and `f"{worst:+.2%}"` crashed the whole stage — before persist, so no book was written. Did not reproduce locally (local book had full betas). Filter `None` estimates before `min()` (default 0.0) + regression test. A full L0→L5 re-run confirms `pipeline_runs` L5 flips `partial → success`. |

| 2026-07-23 | **HypeScore normalization consistency (ADR-0028) + attention-density fix.** `hype_score` fed `volume`/`momentum` min-max'd across themes but `corr` as a **raw `abs()`** (the T22 change) — on a noisy short window `\|corr\|`≈0.1–0.4, so the 30% corr weight structurally under-delivered, compressed the distribution, and pinned themes below the 50 threshold (a likely cause of "no positions"). **ADR-0028**: min-max `abs(corr)` across themes like volume/momentum (supersedes T22); keep `sentiment` absolute because its **sign** is information; `persist()` stores the same min-max `corr_score` so display = score (preserves the PROGRESS-42 reproducibility fix). Live effect: themes clearing 50 went **1 → 2**. Diagnosed the honest caveat: `price_corr` was **degenerate (all 0)** because mentions were only 0–3/day, so the min-max lift came from the 0.5 neutral fallback, not a real corr signal. **Root-cause fix**: Brave `count` 20→50 and the theme↔price **correlation window widened 7→30 days** (`CORR_WINDOW_DAYS`; momentum stays on the trailing 7) so the mention series has enough spread to correlate. ADR-0028 filed. Backend suite **324 passing**. |
| 2026-07-23 | **Correlation was dead since day one — date-type alignment bug fixed (the true ADR-0028 root cause).** After ADR-0028's min-max still showed `price_corr` degenerate (all 0), traced it to `backend/data/yahoo_client.correlation_with_mentions`: it intersected an **ISO-string** mention index (`build_theme_signals` keys the series with `.isoformat()[:10]`) with a **`datetime.date`** price index — the intersection is *always* empty → `<5 common dates` → returned `0.0` for **every theme, every run**. So the 30% correlation weight had never contributed. Fixed by coercing both indices to `datetime.date` before aligning (+ regression test with mismatched index types). Correlation is now **live and varying** (China Growth +0.39, US Dollar +0.26, Inflation −0.35, …) and genuinely discriminates: China Growth leads at hype **82.6** on real corr, not the neutral +15. Net result — a **verified long/short book** persisted (`display_status=verified`, `fallback_used=false`): **LONG FXI + SHORT UUP**, a coherent long-China / short-USD pair citing the new `DFF` (Fed funds) and `DFII10` (real yield) series, with measurable counter-theses. Backend suite **325 passing**. |

| 2026-07-29 | **A mark you can see but cannot name is a mark you report as absent** (`frontend/components/NarrativeTrends.tsx`, [ADR-0162](docs/adrs/0162-a-mark-you-can-see-but-cannot-name.md)) -- a reader asked why `ai` was missing from the detection plane on `/`. It was not missing: at 15.6% share and velocity +2.27 it was the maximum of BOTH axes and therefore set the x-scale, sitting in the top-right alarm corner as an unlabelled 3px hollow ring. ADR-0146 stacked a third distinction on its two good ones -- hollow = context, filled = payload, *and only the payload is labelled* -- so the muted channel was also the anonymous one. Same defect one region down: the rug prints its count and never its members, so `chip` (7.3% of headlines, 7th by share against a 5-row table, `first_seen` that day so genuinely unmeasurable) appeared in NO text anywhere on the board. Fixed: covered marks labelled in `--text-tertiary` against the payload's `--text-secondary` (split lands twice, fill AND ink); caps 6 uncovered / 4 covered through ONE collision pass; leader lines on displaced labels, the rule `TrendPlot` already had, because ten labels instead of six push `us` and `oil prices` 50-60px off their own dots; rug names its loudest four with shares. `chip` in the rug was never a bug (`days_observed: 1` vs `MIN_DAYS_FOR_VELOCITY = 4`, ADR-0066 forbids y = 0) -- the rug just never said so. Verified against the live 2026-07-28 archive board, not fixtures: `docs/captures/2026-07-29/narrative-detection-plane.png`. 4 new tests + 1 rewritten (the one asserting covered marks go unlabelled -- the behaviour deliberately reversed). 870 frontend green |
| 2026-07-29 | **The day's second-loudest phrase was watched by nothing, and Energy Prices was watching it** (`backend/data/brave_client.py`, `tests/backend/test_narrative_tracker.py`) -- `oil` (16 docs, 14.7%) attributed to Energy Prices while `oil prices` (11 docs, 10.1%) attributed to nothing, on a detector whose whole question is what nothing is watching. Two deliberate rules interacting, neither wrong: `prune_subsumed` keeps a unigram and a bigram apart whenever the longer holds under 80% of the shorter's documents (11/16 = 69%, so 5 oil stories never say "prices" -- OPEC/supply framing), and ADR-0128's asymmetry says a ONE-word keyword claims only the one-word phrase, so the alias `oil` cannot reach `oil prices`. `THEME_KEYWORDS` offered `crude oil` and `energy prices` with no bridge between them. Fixed by one two-token alias, `"oil prices"`, which claims only phrases at least as specific as itself. Measured over every phrase ever tracked before adding it, per the rule ADR-0129 set for the stoplist: newly covers `oil price(s)`, `oil prices fall/surge/tumble/keep`, `accelerates oil prices`, `drop oil prices` -- nine phrases, all Energy Prices -- and correctly leaves `prices keep easing` and `elsewhere accelerates oil` uncovered. Inflation keeps `prices` / `price pressure` / `cpi`. This is the CHEAP side of ADR-0128's trade (a false "uncovered" reaches a human's list; a false "covered" vanishes from it silently), so it cost nothing today -- `oil prices` is `established` at +0.32 and the shortlist only fires on emerging AND uncovered -- but on a day oil broke out it would have read as a blind spot the pipeline already covers. Separately confirmed a stale row: `oil price -> Inflation` in the combined corpus predates the asymmetry rule; current code returns None for it. Takes effect on the next daily run; persisted rows are not rewritten. 4 new tests, 1381 backend green |
| 2026-07-29 | **A watch for the state before a narrative has a name** (`scripts/watch_phrases.py`, `backend/services/narrative_tracker.py::document_floor`, `backend/data/brave_client.py`, `tests/backend/test_watch_phrases.py`) -- asked whether AI should be one theme or two. It should be one: a theme here is a narrative driving cross-asset moves through MAPPED instruments, and an "AI" theme would hold SMH/NVDA/TSM/MSFT/GOOGL, every one already in AI Capex's nine-link chain -- two themes over one correlation complex is precisely the double-count ADR-0116 had to be written to survive. But the corpus does contain a second narrative: of the 16 documents behind `ai` at 15.6% on 2026-07-28, roughly half are not the capex chain but the AI trade AS A POSITION ("Big Short investor warns AI has become one dangerous trade", "Why India stock market is the inverse AI trade", "Indian equities as an AI market hedge") -- different instruments, opposite sign. It is invisible not because of attribution (ADR-0128's asymmetry means the token `ai` claims only the phrase `ai`, so `inverse ai trade` is ALREADY uncovered) but because each framing appears in one document against a floor of three. So: don't create the theme, watch it earn one, the way AI Capex did (ADR-0129). The watch reuses `load_market_corpus`/`document_floor`/`phrases_in` -- the first draft assembled its own corpus and was wrong three ways, each of which moves the denominator every number is a ratio against: PostgREST's 1000-row response cap silently truncated 2,309 gdelt rows to 1,000 (ADR-0154), rows were grouped by `run_date` which is the FETCH day (ADR-0158), and headlines were not deduped. `document_floor` extracted from `daily_phrase_counts` for the same reason. Running it immediately surfaced an unrelated live problem: the archive corpus now assembles to **912 documents against the board's last 109, 8.4x past ADR-0155's 2.5x limit**, so the next archive run is on course to withhold every velocity -- the script now says so rather than leaving a reader to divide. Also added `capex guidance` to AI Capex coverage (6.1% share, uncovered, and the documents behind it are Meta/Alphabet/Microsoft capex and "AI capex budgets"); NOT `amd`, because the same week carries "FDA Approval Of Wet AMD Drug" and a wrongly-covered phrase leaves the shortlist silently, nor `cloud`, half of whose headlines are the weather metaphor. 24 new tests, 1405 backend green |
| 2026-07-29 | **The archive corpus never jumped; the watch was reading the wrong date field** (`scripts/watch_phrases.py`, `tests/backend/test_watch_phrases.py`) -- yesterday's watch reported the archive corpus at 912 documents against the board's 109 and predicted the night's velocities would be withheld. Both false. Replaying `load_corpus_by_day` + `replay_archive` read-only reproduces the live series exactly: **1170 rows over 40 publication days, 381 with velocity, 2026-07-28 corpus 109** -- identical to what is persisted. Per-day sizes are stable (min 6, median 49, max 167). The fault was in the watch: the archive series is built by `extend_archive_series` bucketing on **publication date** (ADR-0158), and the watch called `load_market_corpus`, which selects on `run_date` -- the FETCH day. `deepen_archive.py` had loaded 2,309 GDELT rows across two fetch days, so one query returned 45 publication days as if they were one, deduping to 913. That is verbatim the failure `replay_archive`'s own docstring describes ("1449 documents spanning 32 publication days"); the watch had reproduced the bug ADR-0158 exists to fix and raised an alarm about itself. Now each series is watched the way it is built -- archive via `load_corpus_by_day`, combined via `load_market_corpus` -- and the observation is per-day rather than pooled, because the floor is per-day and pooling would report a phrase seen once on each of ten days as 10 against a floor of 3. The comparability check then cried wolf a SECOND time from the opposite end (11 against 109, "0.1x") by comparing the newest publication day, which is structurally the thinnest and back-fills as the provider publishes -- ADR-0159's lesson, arriving through a third door. It now compares on the day the board actually scored and agrees with it exactly (109 vs 109), which doubles as a check that the watch measures what the board measures. Velocity itself needed no change and has none: 381 measured velocities, 38 emerging, and the 11 withheld days are thin publication days correctly refused by ADR-0155's guard, which self-heal as GDELT back-fills them. 5 new tests, 1410 backend green |
